Environmental and Nutritional Pathology

5% of Pathology PLE

▪Environmental Toxicants

Outdoor Air-Pollution–Associated Injury

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossSUGGESTIVE Congested/edematous airways ± hyperinflation; carbon/soot pigmentation may be visible in lungs or hilar nodes
MicroscopyAirway epithelial injury/inflammation ± carbon-laden alveolar macrophages
Confirming stain/markerH&E for inflammation; Prussian blue SUPPLEMENTARY is negative for carbon and helps distinguish hemosiderin
Mimic killerHemosiderin macrophages → Prussian blue positive; carbon pigment is black/brown and iron-negative
Lab/tumor markerNo specific serum marker; pulmonary-function abnormalities may accompany clinically important exposure
Pathognomonic?No — soot-laden macrophages are exposure clues, not diagnostic of one disease

Carbon Monoxide Poisoning

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossHIGHLY CHARACTERISTIC “Cherry-red” skin/tissues in severe poisoning; brain may show bilateral basal-ganglia softening
MicroscopyBilateral liquefactive necrosis of the globus pallidus ± other hypoxic CNS injury
Confirming stain/markerCarboxyhemoglobin by co-oximetry CONFIRMATORY; no diagnostic tissue stain
Mimic killerOther global hypoxic injuries → marked COHb elevation + combustion exposure + bilateral pallidal injury favors CO
Lab/tumor markerCarboxyhemoglobin ↑
Pathognomonic?Rubin calls bilateral globus-pallidus liquefactive necrosis “almost pathognomonic” in severe CO intoxication; it should still be correlated with COHb/exposure
  • Rubin specifically describes red carboxyhemoglobin producing the “cherry-red” hue and severe poisoning producing almost pathognomonic bilateral pallidal liquefactive necrosis.

Lead Poisoning

Rapid-review elementFinding
Gross / radiologicGingival lead line; children may show dense metaphyseal/epiphyseal “lead lines” on radiographs
MicroscopyHIGHLY CHARACTERISTIC Coarse basophilic stippling in microcytic hypochromic RBCs; marrow may show ring sideroblasts
Confirming stain/markerPrussian blue highlights iron-laden mitochondria of ring sideroblasts; blood lead assay confirms exposure
Mimic killerIron-deficiency anemia → lacks basophilic stippling + neurologic/renal toxicity + elevated blood lead
Lab/tumor markerBlood lead ↑; zinc/free erythrocyte protoporphyrin ↑
Pathognomonic?No — basophilic stippling is highly suggestive but occurs in other disorders
  • Robbins describes microcytic hypochromic anemia, distinctive basophilic stippling, ring sideroblasts detectable with Prussian blue, renal proximal-tubular inclusions, and adult wrist/foot drop.

Mercury Poisoning

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossChronic exposure may produce cerebellar atrophy; gingivitis may be visible clinically
MicroscopyPurkinje-cell loss in chronic neurotoxicity; acute inorganic exposure may produce renal tubular necrosis
Confirming stain/markerNo specific histochemical stain; blood/urine mercury SUPPLEMENTARY documents exposure
Mimic killerLead toxicity → lead favors stippled anemia/wrist drop; mercury favors tremor + behavioral change + gingivitis
Lab/tumor markerMercury concentration ↑ in appropriate blood/urine specimen
Pathognomonic?No — Purkinje-cell loss and tubular necrosis are nonspecific
  • Rubin describes chronic inorganic mercury intoxication with neuropsychiatric symptoms, tremor, and cerebellar atrophy with Purkinje-cell loss.

Chronic Arsenic Toxicity

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossHIGHLY CHARACTERISTIC Hyperpigmentation + hyperkeratotic lesions, often multiple on palms and soles
MicroscopyEpidermal hyperkeratosis/acanthosis ± keratinocytic dysplasia; carcinoma shows true dermal invasion
Confirming stain/markerNo arsenic-specific IHC; urinary arsenic SUPPLEMENTARY supports recent exposure
Mimic killerUV-related keratinocytic lesions → arsenic lesions are often multiple and acral (palms/soles)
Lab/tumor markerUrinary arsenic ↑; no exposure-specific tumor marker
Cancer associationSkin, lung, bladder carcinomas
Pathognomonic?Acral hyperkeratoses are characteristic, not pathognomonic
  • Robbins identifies chronic hyperpigmentation/hyperkeratosis and increased skin, lung, and bladder cancer risk; arsenic-related skin tumors are often multiple and occur on palms and soles.

Cadmium Toxicity

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossSkeletal fragility/deformity in severe disease; advanced lungs may be hyperinflated and kidneys scarred
MicroscopyRenal tubular injury + alveolar epithelial necrosis; bone may show osteoporosis plus osteomalacia
Confirming stain/markerNo specific tissue stain; blood/urine cadmium SUPPLEMENTARY supports exposure
Mimic killerSimple nutritional osteomalacia → cadmium adds renal tubular disease + toxic exposure
Lab/tumor markerCadmium ↑ in exposure testing; renal tubular abnormalities may accompany disease
Classic syndromeItai-itai (“ouch-ouch”) disease
NeoplasiaIncreased lung-cancer risk; no cadmium-specific tumor marker
  • Robbins defines itai-itai disease as a combination of osteoporosis and osteomalacia associated with renal disease in cadmium exposure.

▪Nutritional Deficiencies

Marasmus

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossHIGHLY CHARACTERISTIC Severe generalized wasting with loss of fat and muscle, without prominent edema
MicroscopySkeletal/visceral cellular atrophy ± hypoplastic marrow
Confirming stain/markerNo specific stain; nutritional anthropometry/laboratory evaluation confirms severe energy deficiency
Mimic killerKwashiorkor → marasmus lacks marked edema and fatty hepatomegaly
Lab/tumor markerAlbumin relatively better preserved than in kwashiorkor
Pathognomonic?No, but severe nonedematous wasting is highly characteristic

Kwashiorkor

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossGeneralized edema + hepatomegaly + protuberant abdomen; “flaky-paint” dermatosis and hair “flag sign” may occur
MicroscopyProminent hepatocellular fatty change with nucleus displaced peripherally; intestinal villous atrophy may occur
Confirming stain/markerH&E shows steatosis; no specific histochemical diagnostic stain
Mimic killerMarasmus → edema + fatty liver + marked hypoalbuminemia = kwashiorkor
Lab/tumor markerSerum albumin markedly ↓
Pathognomonic?Individual findings are not pathognomonic; the combined phenotype is highly characteristic
  • Rubin describes severe edema, hepatomegaly, “flaky paint” skin lesions, the hair “flag sign,” villous atrophy, and conspicuous fatty liver in kwashiorkor.

Marasmus vs Kwashiorkor — 10-Second Table

FeatureMarasmusKwashiorkor
Main gross clueExtreme wastingEdema
Subcutaneous fatMarkedly ↓Relatively preserved
LiverUsually no striking fatty hepatomegalyFatty enlarged liver
AlbuminRelatively preservedMarkedly ↓
Skin/hairLess characteristicFlaky-paint dermatosis / flag sign
Best discriminatorNo major edemaEdema + fatty liver

Vitamin A Deficiency

Rapid-review elementFinding
Gross / ophthalmicBitot spots → xerophthalmia → corneal ulceration/keratomalacia
MicroscopyKeratinizing squamous metaplasia of specialized glandular/respiratory/urinary epithelium
Confirming stain/markerH&E shows metaplasia; serum retinol ↓ SUPPLEMENTARY
Mimic killerIrritative/smoking metaplasia → vitamin-A deficiency adds ocular disease + multisite epithelial keratinization
Lab/tumor markerSerum retinol ↓ SUPPLEMENTARY
Pathognomonic?Bitot spots and keratomalacia are highly characteristic but not isolated pathognomonic H&E findings
  • Robbins' vitamin-A figure links deficiency with Bitot spots, corneal ulceration, keratomalacia, and keratinizing epithelial metaplasia.
  • Rubin likewise emphasizes squamous metaplasia, xerophthalmia, and keratomalacia.

Vitamin D Deficiency — Rickets

Rapid-review elementFinding
Gross / radiologyBowed legs, widened metaphyses; rachitic rosary, metaphyseal cupping/fraying SUPPLEMENTARY
MicroscopyWidened, irregular, disorganized growth plate with failure of normal mineralization
Confirming stain/markerBone histology; 25-OH vitamin D ↓ SUPPLEMENTARY
Mimic killerOsteomalacia → rickets requires an open growth plate and disorganized growth-plate cartilage
Lab/tumor markerTypically ALP ↑, phosphate ↓, PTH ↑; 25-OH-D ↓ SUPPLEMENTARY
Pathognomonic?Growth-plate pattern is characteristic, interpreted with age/labs

Vitamin D Deficiency — Osteomalacia

Rapid-review elementFinding
Gross / radiologyOsteopenic bone ± Looser/Milkman pseudofractures
MicroscopyThickened seams of unmineralized osteoid along trabeculae
Confirming stain/markerVon Kossa highlights mineralized matrix and makes excess unmineralized osteoid conspicuous
Mimic killerOsteoporosis → osteomalacia = too much unmineralized osteoid; osteoporosis = too little but normally mineralized bone
Lab/tumor marker25-OH-D ↓, ALP ↑, phosphate ↓, PTH ↑ SUPPLEMENTARY
Pathognomonic?Thick osteoid seams are characteristic but cause-nonspecific

Vitamin C Deficiency — Scurvy

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossPerifollicular petechiae/ecchymoses + swollen bleeding gums + poor wound healing
MicroscopyPerifollicular hemorrhage with defective osteoid/collagen formation
Confirming stain/markerNo diagnostic tissue stain; serum ascorbic acid ↓ supports deficiency
Mimic killerPlatelet disorder → scurvy adds gingival disease + perifollicular hemorrhage + impaired wound healing
Lab/tumor markerSerum ascorbate ↓; platelet count may remain normal
Pathognomonic?No, but the combined morphologic pattern is strongly suggestive

Thiamine Deficiency — Dry Beriberi

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossMuscle wasting/weakness; no distinctive gross nerve appearance
MicroscopyPeripheral-nerve myelin degeneration ± later axonal fragmentation
Confirming stain/markerNo specific histologic stain; blood thiamine or erythrocyte transketolase may support diagnosis
Mimic killerOther peripheral neuropathies → nutritional setting + associated Wernicke/cardiac manifestations
Lab/tumor markerThiamine ↓ / transketolase abnormal
Pathognomonic?No — Rubin explicitly states peripheral-nerve changes are not pathognomonic
  • Rubin describes myelin-sheath degeneration as characteristic but specifically states that thiamine-deficient peripheral-nerve morphology is not pathognomonic.

Thiamine Deficiency — Wernicke Encephalopathy

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossHIGHLY CHARACTERISTIC Petechial hemorrhages in mammillary bodies and periventricular anterior thalamus
MicroscopyNeuronal injury/loss, vascular congestion and petechial hemorrhage ± gliosis
Confirming stain/markerH&E demonstrates hemorrhagic lesions; thiamine/transketolase testing supports deficiency
Mimic killerAlcohol-related cerebellar degeneration → Wernicke preferentially involves mammillary/periventricular structures
Lab/tumor markerThiamine ↓; erythrocyte transketolase abnormal
Pathognomonic?Highly characteristic, not absolutely pathognomonic
  • Rubin's neuropathology figure shows hemorrhagic petechiae in the mammillary bodies and periventricular anterior thalamus in Wernicke encephalopathy.

Thiamine Deficiency — Wet Beriberi

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossDilated, flabby enlarged heart + generalized edema
MicroscopyNonspecific myocardial degeneration/edema
Confirming stain/markerNo specific stain; nutritional laboratory assessment
Mimic killerOther cardiomyopathies → concurrent thiamine-deficiency neuropathy/nutritional context
Lab/tumor markerThiamine ↓ / transketolase abnormal
Pathognomonic?No — cardiac morphology is nonspecific

▪Benign vs Malignant — Environmental Cancer Link

CriterionBenign lesionMalignant lesion
BorderCircumscribed/pushingInfiltrative/destructive
CytologyUsually well differentiatedMay show pleomorphism/anaplasia
Basement membranePreserved in epithelial benign/in-situ lesionsBreached in invasive carcinoma
Local invasionAbsentPresent
MetastasisAbsentMay occur; definitive malignancy
Exposure attributionCannot be proven from morphology aloneCannot be proven from morphology alone
  • Arsenic: chronic exposure increases risk of skin, lung, and bladder malignancies; arsenic-induced skin tumors are often multiple and acral.
  • Cadmium: chronic exposure is associated with increased lung-cancer risk.
  • Tumor-marker rule: no serum tumor marker identifies arsenic-, cadmium-, radon-, or pollution-induced cancer as exposure-caused; tumor classification relies on the tumor's usual morphology/IHC.

▪Final 20-Second Board Recall

  • CO: cherry red + bilateral globus pallidus liquefactive necrosis → COHb ↑; pallidal injury is almost pathognomonic, not absolutely exclusive.
  • Lead: basophilic stippling + ring sideroblasts + wrist drop → blood lead ↑; Prussian blue highlights sideroblast iron.
  • Mercury: “mad as a hatter” + tremor/gingivitis + Purkinje-cell loss.
  • Arsenic: hyperpigmentation/hyperkeratosis of palms and soles → skin/lung/bladder cancer risk.
  • Cadmium: renal tubular toxicity + osteoporosis/osteomalacia = itai-itai.
  • Marasmus: wasting without edema.
  • Kwashiorkor: edema + hypoalbuminemia + fatty liver, with flaky-paint skin and flag sign as classic descriptors.
  • Vitamin A: Bitot spots / keratomalacia + keratinizing squamous metaplasia.
  • Rickets: wide disorganized growth plate.
  • Osteomalacia: thick unmineralized osteoid seams; unlike osteoporosis, mineralization itself is defective.
  • Scurvy: perifollicular hemorrhage + bleeding gums + poor wound healing.
  • Wernicke: mammillary-body petechial hemorrhage + confusion/ataxia/ocular findings.

  • Robbins describes genital HSV as red papules progressing to vesicles and painful ulcers, with multinucleated cells and nuclear inclusions showing a ground-glass appearance.

Hepatitis B Virus — HBV

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossAcute liver normal/enlarged; severe necrosis may cause a shrunken liver; chronic disease → nodular cirrhosis
MicroscopyHIGHLY CHARACTERISTIC Ground-glass hepatocytes in chronic HBV; chronic portal inflammation/interface activity
Confirming stain/markerHBsAg and HBcAg immunostains [CONFIRMATORY in tissue]
Mimic killerOther chronic hepatitis → HBV antigen positivity + ground-glass hepatocytes
Lab/tumor markerHBsAg, HBV DNA; acute infection classically anti-HBc IgM SUPPLEMENTARY
Neoplasia markerAFP may ↑ in HCC SUPPLEMENTARY, but is neither sensitive nor specific enough to establish HCC
Pathognomonic?Ground-glass hepatocytes are characteristic, not independently pathognomonic
  • Robbins attributes the ground-glass appearance to hepatocytes filled with HBsAg and states that hepatitis-B surface/core antigen immunostaining confirms HBV infection.

Epstein–Barr Virus — Infectious Mononucleosis

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossEnlarged nodes; soft, enlarged spleen with rupture risk
Microscopy / smearAtypical lymphocytes in blood; reactive paracortical immunoblastic expansion in nodes
Confirming stain/markerHeterophile antibody/EBV-specific serology; EBER ISH SUPPLEMENTARY for tissue
Mimic killerHodgkin lymphoma → EBV mono retains a polymorphous reactive population rather than a clonal malignant architecture
Lab/tumor markerLymphocytosis with atypical lymphocytes; heterophile antibody may be positive
Neoplasia associationEBV-associated B-cell lymphomas, Burkitt lymphoma, nasopharyngeal carcinoma
Pathognomonic?Atypical lymphocytes are characteristic, not EBV-specific

▪Gram-Positive / Gram-Negative Pyogenic Bacteria

Pyogenic Bacterial Abscess

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossCreamy yellow-white pus-filled cavity
MicroscopyCentral liquefactive necrosis with sheets of neutrophils
Confirming stain/markerGram stain + culture CONFIRMATORY
Mimic killerSterile abscess → demonstrable bacteria/culture positivity
LabNeutrophilic leukocytosis, CRP ↑ may accompany infection
Pathognomonic?Suppuration is suggestive of pyogenic infection, not organism-specific

Staphylococcus Aureus

GrossMicroscopyConfirmMimic killerLab
Localized abscessesNeutrophilic liquefactive necrosisGram-positive cocci in clusters + cultureClustered cocci rather than chainsCulture positive

Acute Bacterial Meningitis

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossThick purulent leptomeningeal exudate
MicroscopyNeutrophils fill subarachnoid/leptomeningeal spaces
Confirming stain/markerCSF Gram stain/culture
Mimic killerViral meningitis → lymphocytic rather than neutrophilic CSF
LabCSF neutrophils ↑, protein ↑, glucose ↓, opening pressure ↑
Pathognomonic?Purulent meningitis is highly characteristic of bacterial infection but not species-specific

▪Mycobacterial Infection

Primary Pulmonary Tuberculosis

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossGhon focus = 1–1.5-cm gray-white subpleural focus; focus + caseating regional nodes = Ghon complex
MicroscopyCaseating granuloma with epithelioid histiocytes ± multinucleated giant cells
Confirming stain/markerZiehl–Neelsen / acid-fast stain [CONFIRMATORY when bacilli demonstrated]
Mimic killerFungal granuloma → AFB positive vs fungal GMS/PAS positive
LabAFB smear/culture/NAAT for active disease; TST/IGRA reflects sensitization
Pathognomonic?Caseation is NOT pathognomonic; fungal infections can mimic it
  • Robbins defines the Ghon focus, Ghon complex, and caseating tubercle and specifically advises acid-fast staining whenever granulomas raise suspicion, even when central caseation is absent.

Secondary Pulmonary Tuberculosis

GrossMicroscopyConfirmMimic killerLab
Apical gray-white caseation with softening/cavitationCaseating granulomasAFB stain/culture/NAATApical cavitary granulomatous disease + AFBSputum AFB/NAAT
  • Robbins illustrates secondary TB with upper lungs riddled by caseation, softening and cavitation.

Miliary Tuberculosis

GrossMicroscopyConfirmMimic killerLab
Innumerable tiny millet-seed lesionsDisseminated granulomasAFB stain/cultureHematogenous multisystem distributionMicrobiologic confirmation

▪Spirochetal Infection

Primary Syphilis

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossHIGHLY CHARACTERISTIC Hard chancre = firm, clean-based shallow ulcer with induration
MicroscopyDense plasma-cell infiltrate + proliferative endarteritis
Confirming stain/markerTreponemal testing; organism IHC/silver stain may demonstrate spirochetes
Mimic killerChancroid → syphilitic lesion is classically firm and relatively painless, with plasma-cell endarteritis
LabRPR/VDRL screening/activity + confirmatory treponemal test
Pathognomonic?Neither chancre nor plasma-cell infiltrate alone is pathognomonic
  • Robbins describes the chancre as a firm reddened papule evolving into a clean-based shallow ulcer, with intense plasma-cell infiltration and proliferative endarteritis.

Secondary Syphilis

GrossMicroscopyConfirmMimic killerLab
Red-brown rash including palms and solesPlasma cells + obliterative/proliferative endarteritisTreponemal testingPalm/sole rash plus syphilitic vascular morphologySerology highly sensitive in secondary stage

Tertiary Syphilis — Gumma / Aortitis

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossWhite-gray rubbery gumma; hepatic scarring → hepar lobatum
MicroscopyNecrotic inflammatory lesion rich in plasma cells; aortitis shows vasa-vasorum endarteritis and medial scarring
Confirming stain/markerTreponemal serology; organisms often sparse in tertiary lesions
Mimic killerTB granuloma → syphilis favors plasma cells/endarteritis rather than classic caseating tubercle
LabTreponemal tests remain positive; nontreponemal titers may fall with time
Pathognomonic?Gumma is highly characteristic but requires clinicoserologic confirmation
  • Robbins describes white-gray rubbery gummas, hepatic hepar lobatum, and vasa-vasorum endarteritis in tertiary disease.

▪Anaerobic Infection

Clostridial Gas Gangrene — C. Perfringens

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossHIGHLY CHARACTERISTIC Soft, blue-black, friable/semifluid muscle + edema + bullae + gas/crepitus
MicroscopySevere myonecrosis, hemolysis and vascular thrombosis with surprisingly few inflammatory cells
Confirming stain/markerGram stain: large boxcar-shaped gram-positive bacilli + culture
Mimic killerPyogenic necrotizing infection → gas + massive necrosis disproportionate to neutrophils
LabCulture; hemolysis may cause hemoglobin ↓
Neoplasia associationSpontaneous C. septicum infection strongly suggests possible underlying malignancy
Pathognomonic?Combined gross/micro pattern is highly characteristic; organism demonstration confirms
  • Robbins describes boxcar-shaped gram-positive bacilli, necrosis out of proportion to neutrophils, and blue-black friable muscle with gas in clostridial gangrene.

▪Chlamydial Infection

Chlamydia Trachomatis — Cervicitis / Salpingitis

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossMucopurulent cervicitis; chronic tubal disease may show thickening/scarring
MicroscopyMixed neutrophilic/lymphocytic inflammation ± lymphoid aggregates; cytoplasmic inclusions may be present
Confirming stain/markerNAAT SUPPLEMENTARY, CONFIRMATORY
Mimic killerGonococcal cervicitis → routine histology overlaps; NAAT identifies Chlamydia
LabChlamydial NAAT positive
ComplicationTubal fibrosis → infertility/ectopic pregnancy
Pathognomonic?Intracytoplasmic inclusions are characteristic when seen but are not sufficiently sensitive for diagnosis

Lymphogranuloma Venereum

GrossMicroscopyConfirmMimic killerLab
Small transient lesion → suppurative inguinal nodes/fistulasNecrotizing/suppurative granulomatous inflammationChlamydial NAAT/serology as applicableInvasive lymphatic disease patternOrganism-specific testing

▪Rickettsial Infection

Rocky Mountain Spotted Fever

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossPetechial rash involving palms and soles
MicroscopySmall-vessel endothelial injury/necrosis, perivascular inflammation, thrombosis and hemorrhage
Confirming stain/markerGimenez stain may demonstrate organisms; serologic/molecular testing confirms
Mimic killerImmune vasculitis → tick exposure + organism-specific testing
LabSerology/PCR as applicable; thrombocytopenia may accompany severe disease
Pathognomonic?Palm/sole rash and vasculitis are suggestive, not pathognomonic

▪Fungal Infections

Candida

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossWhite-gray adherent pseudomembranes / thrush
MicroscopyBudding yeast + pseudohyphae with constrictions
Confirming stain/markerGMS or PAS [CONFIRMATORY when invasive organisms are demonstrated]
Mimic killerAspergillus → Candida has budding yeast/pseudohyphae, not uniform septate acute-angle hyphae
LabCulture/organism-specific tests; tissue invasion distinguishes infection from colonization
Pathognomonic?Pseudohyphae + budding yeast are highly characteristic; tissue invasion proves invasive candidiasis

Aspergillus

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossFungus ball (aspergilloma) in old cavity; invasive disease may show hemorrhagic target-like lesions
MicroscopyNarrow septate hyphae with acute-angle (~45°) branching
Confirming stain/markerGMS/PAS highlights hyphae
Mimic killerMucor → Aspergillus = septate + acute angle
LabCulture; fungal antigen testing SUPPLEMENTARY
ComplicationAngioinvasion → thrombosis → hemorrhagic infarction
Pathognomonic?Hyphal morphology is highly characteristic, but culture/molecular correlation is ideal

Mucormycosis

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossBlack/necrotic sinonasal-orbital tissue from vascular infarction
MicroscopyBroad irregular pauciseptate/nonseptate hyphae with near-right-angle branching
Confirming stain/markerGMS/PAS
Mimic killerAspergillus → Mucor = broad + pauciseptate + right-angle
LabHistologic invasion/culture; no routine serum tumor marker
Classic settingDiabetic ketoacidosis or profound immunosuppression
Pathognomonic?Morphology is very characteristic; vascular/tissue invasion establishes invasive infection

Fungal 3-Way Discriminator

OrganismYeast / hyphaeSeptationBranching
CandidaBudding yeast + pseudohyphaeConstricted pseudohyphaeIrregular
AspergillusTrue narrow hyphaeSeptateAcute angle
MucorBroad ribbon-like hyphaePauciseptate / nonseptateRight/obtuse angle

▪Parasitic Infections

Malaria

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossChronic disease → enlarged dark spleen and liver
MicroscopyIntraerythrocytic parasites; tissues contain brown-black hemozoin pigment
Confirming stain/markerGiemsa-stained thick/thin peripheral smear CONFIRMATORY
Mimic killerBabesiosis → Maltese-cross tetrads and no hemozoin
LabParasitemia; anemia/thrombocytopenia may occur
Pathognomonic?Demonstration of characteristic Plasmodium forms on smear is diagnostic; splenomegaly/hemozoin alone is not

Cerebral Falciparum Malaria

GrossMicroscopyConfirmMimic killerLab
Brain congestion/edema ± petechiaeCapillaries packed with parasitized RBCs ± ring hemorrhagesGiemsa smearSequestered parasitized RBCsHigh parasitemia

Amebiasis — Entamoeba Histolytica

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossHIGHLY CHARACTERISTIC Flask-shaped colonic ulcers with narrow mucosal opening and broad undermined submucosal extension
MicroscopyTrophozoites at ulcer margin; trophozoites containing ingested RBCs HIGHLY SPECIFIC
Confirming stain/markerDemonstration of trophozoites in tissue; stool antigen/PCR SUPPLEMENTARY
Mimic killerMacrophages → ingested RBCs within true trophozoites strongly favor E. histolytica
LabStool/tissue testing; no tumor marker
Pathognomonic?Erythrophagocytic trophozoites are highly diagnostic; flask shape alone is characteristic but not sufficient

Amebic Liver Abscess

GrossMicroscopyConfirmMimic killerLab
Large necrotic hepatic cavityNecrosis with trophozoites mainly at viable edgeOrganism demonstration/serology SUPPLEMENTARYTrophozoites at abscess marginAmebic testing

Schistosomiasis

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossChronic liver/bladder disease → fibrosis ± calcification
MicroscopyEgg-centered granulomas rich in eosinophils, later fibrosis/calcification
Confirming stain/markerIdentification of characteristic eggs in tissue/stool/urine CONFIRMATORY
Mimic killerTB/fungal granuloma → visible helminth egg + eosinophils
LabPeripheral eosinophilia may occur; eggs in stool/urine
Pathognomonic?Species-characteristic egg morphology can be diagnostic; eosinophilic granuloma alone is not

▪Infection-Associated Neoplasia

InfectionAssociated tumorBenign / malignantSlide/lab clueTumor markerSpread / criterion
HBVHepatocellular carcinomaMalignantHepatocellular malignant morphology + chronic HBV backgroundAFP may ↑ but is imperfectVascular invasion/hematogenous
EBVBurkitt lymphomaMalignantMonomorphic lymphoma; EBV may be demonstratedNo universal serum markerLymphoid dissemination
EBVNasopharyngeal carcinomaMalignantCarcinoma with lymphoid-rich background; EBER may support EBV associationNone routinely diagnosticCervical lymphatic spread
EBVImmunodeficiency-associated B-cell lymphomaMalignantDestructive clonal lymphoid proliferationNo universal serum markerNodal/extranodal
C. septicum associationUnderlying colorectal/hematologic malignancy may coexistDepends on tumorInfection is a warning clue, not a tumor markerNoneStandard tumor criteria
  • Robbins documents strong EBV associations with several lymphoid neoplasms; infection association itself does not establish malignancy.
  • AFP elevation is supportive, not diagnostic, for HCC; morphology/imaging remain necessary.

▪Benign vs Malignant — Infectious Lesion Trap

FeatureReactive / infectious lesionMalignant neoplasm
Cell populationUsually polymorphousOften monomorphic/clonal
ArchitectureReactive distortion but underlying pattern may remainDestructive effacement/invasion
Cytologic atypiaImmunoblasts may look strikingly atypicalPersistent neoplastic atypia/clonality
NecrosisCommon in infectionAlso possible; not diagnostic
MetastasisNot applicableDefinitive malignant behavior
Best discriminatorOrganism/reactive patternClonal/invasive neoplastic growth
  • Classic trap: EBV infectious mononucleosis may contain Reed–Sternberg–like immunoblasts and mimic lymphoma.
  • Classic trap: granulomatous mass ≠ neoplasm; always search for organisms before interpreting a necrotizing mass as cancer.
  • Classic trap: an infection-associated virus does not prove that a patient's mass is malignant.

▪Final 20-Second Board Recall

  • HSV: vesicle + multinucleation + nuclear inclusions → PCR; H&E does not reliably separate HSV from VZV.
  • HBV: ground-glass hepatocyte → HBsAg/HBcAg.
  • EBV: atypical lymphocytes are activated host T cells; beware lymphoma mimic.
  • Pyogenic bacteria: pus = neutrophils + liquefactive necrosis.
  • TB: Ghon complex / caseating granuloma → AFB stain; caseation alone is not pathognomonic.
  • Syphilis: hard chancre + plasma cells + endarteritis → serology.
  • Clostridium: gas + blue-black friable muscle + scant neutrophils + boxcar gram-positive rods.
  • RMSF: palm/sole petechiae + endothelial vasculitis.
  • Candida: budding yeast + pseudohyphae.
  • Aspergillus: acute-angle septate.
  • Mucor: broad right-angle pauciseptate/nonseptate.
  • Malaria: Giemsa-positive intra-RBC parasite + hemozoin.
  • Amebiasis: flask-shaped ulcer + RBC-containing trophozoite.
  • Schistosomiasis: egg + eosinophilic granuloma → chronic fibrosis.
  • Tumor-marker rule: HBV/EBV infection can predispose to malignancy, but viral positivity or AFP elevation never substitutes for demonstration of malignant morphology, invasion, or metastasis.

Regional block · congenital GI abnormalities; esophageal…

Gastrointestinal Diseases

10% of Pathology PLE

▪Congenital / Developmental Gi Disorders

Congenital Diaphragmatic Hernia

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossAbdominal viscera herniate into thorax with small hypoplastic ipsilateral lung
MicroscopyPulmonary hypoplasia; herniated GI organs themselves may be histologically normal
Confirming stain/markerNo specific stain; prenatal/neonatal imaging confirms anatomy
Mimic killerDiaphragmatic eventration → true hernia has an actual diaphragmatic defect
Lab/tumor markerHypoxemia; no disease-specific serum marker
Pathognomonic?Intrathoracic abdominal viscera through a true defect are anatomically diagnostic

Omphalocele

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossMidline umbilical herniation covered by a translucent membrane/sac, containing bowel ± liver
MicroscopyBowel usually normal; herniated viscera remain covered by membranous layers
Confirming stain/markerNo specific stain; prenatal imaging
Mimic killerGastroschisis = uncovered bowel; omphalocele = membrane-covered
Lab/tumor markerNo specific lab; assess associated congenital/chromosomal abnormalities
Pathognomonic?Covering sac + midline umbilical defect is diagnostic anatomically

Meckel Diverticulum

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossBlind-ending pouch from antimesenteric ileum
MicroscopyTrue diverticulum with all bowel-wall layers ± ectopic gastric/pancreatic tissue
Confirming stain/markerTc-99m pertechnetate scan SUPPLEMENTARY detects ectopic gastric mucosa
Mimic killerAcquired diverticulum → lacks complete muscular wall; Meckel is a true diverticulum
Lab/tumor markerBleeding may cause iron-deficiency anemia
Pathognomonic?Antimesenteric true ileal diverticulum is highly characteristic
  • Rubin specifically describes Meckel diverticulum as a true antimesenteric ileal diverticulum containing all intestinal coats, with ectopic gastric tissue explaining adjacent peptic ulceration and bleeding.

Hypertrophic Pyloric Stenosis

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossFirm thickened pylorus forming palpable “olive-shaped” mass
MicroscopyMarked hypertrophy/hyperplasia of pyloric muscularis propria
Confirming stain/markerNo stain required; ultrasound confirms muscular thickening
Mimic killerAcquired gastric-outlet obstruction → infantile disease has primary smooth-muscle hypertrophy without tumor/scar
LabHypochloremic, hypokalemic metabolic alkalosis SUPPLEMENTARY after prolonged vomiting
Pathognomonic?Morphology is highly characteristic, but diagnosis is clinical/imaging based

Hirschsprung Disease

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossNarrow distal aganglionic segment → transition zone → proximal megacolon
MicroscopyAbsence of ganglion cells in submucosal/myenteric plexuses ± hypertrophied nerve trunks
Confirming stain/markerRectal suction biopsy; calretinin absent / AChE increased SUPPLEMENTARY
Mimic killerAcquired megacolon → Hirschsprung has developmental distal aganglionosis
LabNo specific serum marker
Pathognomonic?Absent ganglion cells in an adequate distal rectal biopsy = diagnostic

▪Esophagus

Esophageal Achalasia

Rapid-review elementFinding
Gross/imagingDilated esophagus with distal “bird-beak” tapering
MicroscopyDecreased/absent myenteric ganglion cells ± chronic inflammation
Confirming testEsophageal manometry
Mimic killerPseudoachalasia from carcinoma → mechanical infiltrating mass rather than isolated neuronal loss
Lab/tumor markerNo specific serum marker
Pathognomonic?Bird-beak morphology is characteristic, not unique

GERD / Reflux Esophagitis

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossDistal esophageal erythema ± superficial erosions
MicroscopyBasal-zone hyperplasia + elongated lamina-propria papillae + intraepithelial eosinophils
Confirming stain/markerH&E; no disease-specific IHC
Mimic killerEosinophilic esophagitis → typically much denser eosinophilic infiltration and characteristic ringed esophagus
LabNo specific serum abnormality
Pathognomonic?Histologic triad is suggestive/characteristic, not pathognomonic

Esophageal Varices

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossTortuous, dilated bluish submucosal veins in distal esophagus
MicroscopyLarge thin-walled dilated venous channels immediately beneath squamous mucosa
Confirming testEndoscopy
Mimic killerOrdinary submucosal veins → marked dilation/tortuosity + portal-hypertension context
LabAcute rupture → hemoglobin/hematocrit ↓
Pathognomonic?Morphology is highly characteristic in portal hypertension
  • Robbins illustrates tortuous esophageal varices and dilated veins immediately beneath intact squamous mucosa.

Barrett Esophagus

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossTongues of red, velvety mucosa extending proximally from GE junction
MicroscopyIntestinal-type columnar metaplasia with goblet cells replacing squamous mucosa
Confirming stain/markerH&E; Alcian blue SUPPLEMENTARY highlights acidic goblet-cell mucin
Mimic killerGastric cardia mucosa → Barrett requires intestinal goblet cells in esophageal mucosa
Lab/tumor markerNo serum marker; endoscopic biopsy establishes diagnosis
Pathognomonic?Goblet-cell intestinal metaplasia in the appropriate esophageal location is diagnostic
  • Robbins describes Barrett esophagus as red velvety tongues containing abundant metaplastic goblet cells and distinguishes it from residual pale squamous mucosa.

Esophageal Squamous Cell Carcinoma

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossPolypoid, ulcerative, or infiltrative constricting esophageal mass
MicroscopyInvasive malignant squamous nests ± keratin pearls/intercellular bridges
Confirming stain/markerp40/p63, cytokeratin SUPPLEMENTARY
Mimic killerSevere squamous dysplasia/CIS → stromal invasion proves invasive SCC
Tumor markerNo reliable serum marker
Malignant criterionDestructive invasion ± nodal/distant metastasis

Esophageal Adenocarcinoma

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossDistal esophageal ulcerated/infiltrative mass, often arising in Barrett mucosa
MicroscopyInfiltrating malignant mucin-forming glands in desmoplastic stroma
Confirming stain/markerCytokeratin ± mucin stains SUPPLEMENTARY
Mimic killerBarrett high-grade dysplasia → stromal invasion establishes adenocarcinoma
Tumor markerNo useful diagnostic serum marker
Malignant criterionInvasion/metastasis; stage > grade for prognosis
  • Robbins identifies adenocarcinoma and SCC as the major esophageal cancers and emphasizes the association between Barrett esophagus and esophageal adenocarcinoma.

▪Stomach

Acute Gastritis / Erosive Gastropathy

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossHyperemic gastric mucosa with multiple superficial erosions ± petechial hemorrhage
MicroscopySurface epithelial injury/necrosis; neutrophils establish acute gastritis
Confirming stain/markerH&E
Mimic killerPeptic ulcer → ulcer extends through muscularis mucosae, erosions do not
LabSignificant bleeding → Hb/Hct ↓
Pathognomonic?Erosive morphology is nonspecific

Helicobacter Pylori Gastritis

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossOften subtle erythema/nodularity; may coexist with peptic ulcer
MicroscopyCurved organisms in surface mucus + chronic-active gastritis + lymphoid aggregates/germinal centers
Confirming stain/markerWarthin-Starry, H. pylori IHC; urea breath/fecal antigen tests
Mimic killerAutoimmune gastritis → H. pylori shows surface organisms and often antral/multifocal inflammation
LabUrea-breath or fecal-antigen positive
Pathognomonic?Demonstration of organisms in appropriate gastric tissue confirms infection

Peptic Ulcer Disease

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossRound/oval punched-out crater with clean base and radiating mucosal folds
MicroscopyLayered base: necrotic debris → neutrophils → granulation tissue → fibrous scar
Confirming stain/markerH&E; test for H. pylori when relevant
Mimic killerUlcerated gastric carcinoma → heaped irregular margins + infiltrating malignant cells
LabBleeding → Hb ↓; H. pylori testing may be positive
Pathognomonic?Gross pattern is highly characteristic, not absolutely pathognomonic

Zollinger-Ellison Syndrome / Gastrinoma

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossThick gastric folds with multiple/recurrent ulcers, including distal duodenum/jejunum
MicroscopyGastric parietal-cell hyperplasia; gastrinoma shows nested/trabecular neuroendocrine cells
Confirming stain/markerChromogranin, synaptophysin SUPPLEMENTARY for tumor
Mimic killerOrdinary PUD → multiple distal ulcers + marked hypergastrinemia
Lab/tumor markerFasting serum gastrin markedly ↑
Malignant?Gastrinomas have malignant potential; metastasis establishes malignant behavior

Gastric Adenocarcinoma — Intestinal Type

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossExophytic/ulcerated mass with irregular heaped-up margins
MicroscopyCohesive malignant gland formation
Confirming stain/markerCytokeratin; HER2 in selected tumors SUPPLEMENTARY
Mimic killerPeptic ulcer → invasive malignant glands
Tumor markerCEA/CA19-9 may ↑ but are not diagnostic
Malignant criterionStromal invasion, lymph-node/distant metastasis

Diffuse Gastric Adenocarcinoma

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossLinitis plastica — “leather-bottle stomach” with rigid thickened wall and loss of rugae
MicroscopyDiscohesive signet-ring cells infiltrating wall with desmoplasia
Confirming stain/markerCytokeratin/mucin stains SUPPLEMENTARY
Mimic killerReactive signet-ring-like change → destructive infiltrative malignant growth
Tumor markerNo sufficiently sensitive/specific serum marker
Pathognomonic?Linitis plastica + signet cells is highly characteristic, not exclusively gastric

▪Intestinal Obstruction

Incarcerated / Strangulated Hernia

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossEntrapped bowel becomes dusky red-purple → black/gangrenous if strangulated
MicroscopyCongestion/hemorrhage → mucosal necrosis → transmural ischemic necrosis
Confirming testSurgical/imaging findings; no specific stain
Mimic killerReducible hernia → no ischemic bowel injury
LabLactate may ↑ with ischemia SUPPLEMENTARY
Pathognomonic?Ischemic change confirms strangulation but not the specific hernia type

Volvulus

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossTwisted, massively dilated bowel loop ± dark hemorrhagic/gangrenous wall
MicroscopyIschemic mucosal necrosis progressing to transmural infarction
Confirming testImaging; sigmoid volvulus may show coffee-bean sign SUPPLEMENTARY
Mimic killerIntussusception → twisting around mesentery vs telescoping
LabLactate ↑ if ischemic
Pathognomonic?Twisted mesenteric configuration is anatomically diagnostic

Intussusception

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossTelescoping of proximal bowel into distal bowel, with congested hemorrhagic intussusceptum
MicroscopyEdema/hemorrhage → ischemic mucosal or transmural necrosis
Confirming testUltrasound/contrast-air enema
Mimic killerVolvulus → telescoping vs axial twisting
LabNo specific marker; bleeding may occur
Classic clueCurrant-jelly stool SUPPLEMENTARY from mucus + blood

▪Malabsorption / Diarrhea

Celiac Disease

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossFlattened/scalloped proximal small-intestinal mucosa
MicroscopyVillous atrophy + crypt hyperplasia + increased intraepithelial lymphocytes
Confirming stain/markerH&E; CD3 SUPPLEMENTARY highlights IELs
Mimic killerTropical sprue → celiac favors proximal predominance + celiac serology
LabtTG-IgA / EMA positive SUPPLEMENTARY, iron deficiency common
Pathognomonic?Histologic triad is characteristic, not unique

Diarrheal Patterns

TypeGross / microKey laboratory/clinical discriminator
SecretoryOften normal mucosaPersists during fasting
OsmoticOften normal mucosaImproves during fasting
MalabsorptiveMay show villous abnormalitiesSteatorrhea/nutrient deficiency
InflammatoryErosions/ulcers; neutrophils/cryptitisBlood, mucus, fecal inflammatory markers SUPPLEMENTARY

C. Difficile Pseudomembranous Colitis

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossMultiple adherent yellow-white pseudomembranous plaques on erythematous colon
Microscopy“Volcano lesion” SUPPLEMENTARY with fibrin, mucus, neutrophils and necrotic debris erupting from crypts
Confirming testStool toxin/NAAT
Mimic killerIschemic pseudomembranous colitis → toxin positivity + antibiotic/healthcare context
LabLeukocytosis; stool toxin/NAAT positive
Pathognomonic?Pseudomembranes are not specific for C. difficile

Irritable Bowel Syndrome

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossNormal bowel/endoscopy
MicroscopyEssentially normal mucosa; no chronic destructive architectural lesion
Confirming stain/markerNone
Mimic killerIBD → objective mucosal inflammation/architectural damage
LabNo characteristic inflammatory abnormality
Pathognomonic?Diagnosis depends on clinical criteria/exclusion; no pathognomonic pathology

▪Inflammatory Bowel Disease

Crohn Disease

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossSkip lesions + deep serpentine ulcers + cobblestoning + thickened wall/strictures ± creeping fat
MicroscopyTransmural inflammation, crypt injury/distortion ± noncaseating granulomas
Confirming stain/markerH&E; no single specific IHC
Mimic killerUC → Crohn has skip lesions + transmural disease + fistulas/strictures
LabCRP/calprotectin may ↑ SUPPLEMENTARY; ASCA is supportive but imperfect
Pathognomonic?Granulomas are characteristic but neither required nor pathognomonic
  • Robbins describes Crohn disease with sharply separated skip lesions, serpentine ulcers, cobblestoning, transmural thickening, strictures and creeping fat.

Ulcerative Colitis

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossContinuous rectum-first red granular/friable mucosa + broad superficial ulcers ± pseudopolyps
MicroscopyMucosal cryptitis, crypt abscesses, crypt distortion/atrophy; no true Crohn-type granulomas
Confirming stain/markerH&E
Mimic killerCrohn → continuous superficial mucosal disease without fistulas/strictures/granulomas
LabCRP/calprotectin may ↑; p-ANCA supportive but nonspecific
Pathognomonic?Crypt abscesses are characteristic but not specific
  • Robbins and Rubin emphasize mucosal-limited inflammation, crypt abscesses and chronic crypt distortion in UC, in contrast with transmural Crohn disease.

Crohn vs UC — 10-Second Discriminator

FeatureCrohnUC
DistributionSkipContinuous
RectumVariableUsually involved
DepthTransmuralMucosal/superficial submucosal
GrossCobblestonePseudopolyps/friability
GranulomasMay occurAbsent
FistulaYesNo
StrictureCommonRare
Toxic megacolonPossibleClassic severe complication
SurgeryNot reliably curativeColectomy cures intestinal disease

▪Polyps and Colorectal Neoplasia

Hyperplastic Polyp

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossSmall smooth sessile polyp
MicroscopyMature goblet/absorptive cells with superficial serrated “saw-tooth” architecture
Confirming stain/markerH&E
Mimic killerSessile serrated lesion → hyperplastic polyp lacks abnormal basal crypt dilation/lateral growth
Lab/tumor markerNone
Benign?Benign/nonneoplastic in conventional form

Sessile Serrated Lesion

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossBroad-based, flat/sessile proximal colonic lesion
MicroscopySerration extending to crypt base with dilated/distorted horizontally growing basal crypts
Confirming stain/markerH&E
Mimic killerHyperplastic polyp → basal architectural distortion
Lab/tumor markerNone
Malignant potentialPremalignant, despite often bland cytology

Conventional Adenoma

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossPedunculated tubular lesion or broad velvety villous lesion
MicroscopyDysplastic glands with elongated hyperchromatic pseudostratified nuclei; tubular and/or villous architecture
Confirming stain/markerH&E
Mimic killerHyperplastic polyp → true epithelial dysplasia
Lab/tumor markerNone
Benign vs malignantAdenoma is benign but premalignant; submucosal invasion = invasive carcinoma

Colorectal Adenocarcinoma

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossRight: exophytic/polypoid; left: circumferential “apple-core/napkin-ring” lesion SUPPLEMENTARY
MicroscopyInvasive irregular malignant glands ± dirty necrosis SUPPLEMENTARY and desmoplasia
Confirming stain/markerCK20/CDX2 SUPPLEMENTARY support colorectal lineage
Mimic killerHigh-grade adenoma → invasion into submucosa establishes invasive colorectal carcinoma
Tumor markerCEA mainly for surveillance; not a diagnostic screening test
Malignant criterionSubmucosal invasion ± nodal/hepatic metastasis

▪Small-Intestinal Tumors

TumorGrossMicroscopic hallmarkConfirming markerMimic killerLab/tumor marker
Small-bowel adenocarcinomaAnnular/ulcerated obstructing massInvasive malignant glandsCytokeratinStromal invasionNo reliable serum marker
Neuroendocrine tumorFirm yellow-tan submucosal noduleNested/trabecular cells with salt-and-pepper chromatinChromogranin, synaptophysin SUPPLEMENTARYNeuroendocrine morphology/IHC5-HIAA only in functional syndrome SUPPLEMENTARY
GISTFleshy intramural mass ± hemorrhage/necrosisSpindle/epithelioid stromal tumorKIT/CD117, DOG1 SUPPLEMENTARYSmooth muscle tumor favors desminNo serum tumor marker

▪Appendix / Anorectal Disease

Acute Appendicitis

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossSwollen hyperemic appendix with dull granular serosa ± fibrinopurulent exudate
MicroscopyNeutrophils infiltrating muscularis propria
Confirming stain/markerH&E
Mimic killerSuperficial periappendicitis → lacks diagnostic muscularis-propria neutrophils
LabNeutrophilic leukocytosis common
Pathognomonic?Muscularis-propria neutrophilic infiltration is the key diagnostic histologic criterion

Hemorrhoids

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossBulging blue-purple congested vascular cushions, sometimes thrombosed
MicroscopyDilated thin-walled submucosal veins ± thrombosis/organization
Confirming stain/markerH&E; no special stain
Mimic killerPortal-hypertensive anorectal varices → hemorrhoids are common anal vascular cushions and are not synonymous with portal varices
LabChronic bleeding rarely → anemia
Pathognomonic?Morphology is characteristic but nonspecific

▪Liver

  • Scope note: the source TOS states broadly “diseases of the liver, biliary tract and pancreas” rather than listing individual hepatic entities; the following preserve the representative entities used in the full module.

Hepatic Steatosis / Steatohepatitis

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossEnlarged yellow, soft, greasy liver
MicroscopyMacrovesicular steatosis; steatohepatitis adds ballooning + inflammation ± fibrosis
Confirming stain/markerH&E; Oil Red O/Sudan SUPPLEMENTARY on frozen tissue
Mimic killerMicrovesicular steatosis → many tiny droplets without one dominant nucleus-displacing vacuole
LabAST/ALT may ↑
Pathognomonic?Steatosis morphology identifies fat accumulation, not its cause

Cirrhosis

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossFirm diffusely nodular liver with fibrous septa
MicroscopyRegenerative nodules surrounded by bridging fibrosis with architectural distortion
Confirming stain/markerMasson trichrome SUPPLEMENTARY highlights collagen
Mimic killerNodular regenerative hyperplasia → nodules without significant bridging fibrosis
LabAlbumin ↓, INR ↑, platelets ↓ in advanced disease
Pathognomonic?Diffuse nodularity + bridging fibrosis is diagnostic of cirrhosis morphology, not etiology

Hepatocellular Carcinoma

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossLarge solitary mass, multifocal nodules, or infiltrative tumor ± yellow-green coloration/hemorrhage
MicroscopyThick malignant hepatocellular trabeculae ± bile production and vascular invasion
Confirming stain/markerArginase-1, HepPar-1, glypican-3 SUPPLEMENTARY
Mimic killerMetastatic adenocarcinoma → HCC favors hepatocellular trabeculae/bile production + hepatocellular IHC
Tumor markerAFP may ↑ but lacks adequate sensitivity/specificity
Malignant criterionVascular invasion/metastasis

▪Biliary Tract

Acute / Chronic Cholecystitis

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossAcute: enlarged tense congested gallbladder ± stones; chronic: thick fibrotic wall
MicroscopyAcute = neutrophils/edema; chronic = chronic inflammation + fibrosis ± Rokitansky-Aschoff sinuses SUPPLEMENTARY
Confirming stain/markerH&E; ultrasound identifies stones
Mimic killerGallbladder carcinoma → atypical destructive epithelial invasion
LabLeukocytosis in acute disease; bilirubin/ALP rise if broader obstruction
Pathognomonic?Rokitansky-Aschoff sinuses are characteristic, not exclusive

Primary Sclerosing Cholangitis

Rapid-review elementFinding
Gross/imagingAlternating strictures/dilatations → “beaded” biliary tree SUPPLEMENTARY
MicroscopyConcentric “onion-skin” periductal fibrosis → duct obliteration
Confirming testCholangiographic pattern + biopsy when needed
Mimic killerPBC → PSC = concentric periductal fibrosis of larger ducts; PBC = granulomatous florid duct lesion
LabALP ↑; cholestatic profile
Neoplasia linkIncreased cholangiocarcinoma risk
Pathognomonic?Onion-skin fibrosis is highly characteristic but not present in every specimen

▪Pancreas

Acute Pancreatitis

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossSwollen pancreas with red-black hemorrhage + yellow-white chalky fat necrosis
MicroscopyAcinar necrosis + fat necrosis/saponification + neutrophils + edema + vascular hemorrhage
Confirming stain/markerH&E; no specific IHC
Mimic killerPancreatic adenocarcinoma-associated pancreatitis → look for infiltrating malignant glands/desmoplasia
LabSerum lipase ↑ ± amylase ↑; severe fat necrosis may cause Ca²⁺ ↓
Pathognomonic?Saponifying fat necrosis is highly characteristic, not unique to one etiology

Chronic Pancreatitis

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossHard fibrotic pancreas with irregular dilated ducts ± calcifications
MicroscopyAcinar loss + irregular fibrosis, distorted ducts; islets relatively preserved early
Confirming stain/markerH&E; trichrome SUPPLEMENTARY highlights fibrosis
Mimic killerPancreatic carcinoma → chronic pancreatitis lacks invasive atypical glands/perineural invasion
LabAmylase/lipase may be normal in advanced disease; exocrine insufficiency → steatorrhea
Pathognomonic?Morphology is characteristic but etiology requires clinical context

Pancreatic Ductal Adenocarcinoma

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossPoorly defined, firm gray-white infiltrative mass, often in pancreatic head
MicroscopyIrregular malignant glands in dense desmoplastic stroma ± perineural invasion
Confirming stain/markerCytokeratin; CA19-9 IHC SUPPLEMENTARY may support phenotype but is not diagnostic
Mimic killerChronic pancreatitis → carcinoma shows atypical infiltrative glands/perineural or vascular invasion
Tumor markerSerum CA19-9 useful mainly for monitoring; cholestasis can elevate it
Malignant criterionDestructive invasion, perineural/vascular invasion, metastasis

▪Benign vs Malignant — Gi Board Rules

FeatureBenign / premalignantMalignant
BorderCircumscribed / noninvasiveInfiltrative/destructive
Basement membranePreserved in noninvasive epithelial lesionBreached
Colon adenomaDysplastic but confinedSubmucosal invasion = carcinoma
Barrett dysplasiaAtypical but noninvasiveStromal invasion = adenocarcinoma
Gastric ulcerClean base; no malignant cellsInfiltrating glands/signet cells
GISTRisk spectrumIncreasing size/mitoses/site risk; metastasis definitive
MetastasisAbsentDefinitive malignant behavior
Tumor markerMay be normal or nonspecifically elevatedNever proves malignancy by itself
  • Board priority: in GI epithelial lesions, invasion matters more than cytologic ugliness alone.
  • CEA, AFP, CA19-9 and similar markers are adjuncts/monitoring tools—not replacements for morphology and staging.

▪Final 20-Second Board Recall

  • Omphalocele = covered; gastroschisis = uncovered.
  • Meckel = antimesenteric true diverticulum ± ectopic gastric mucosa.
  • Pyloric stenosis = olive + nonbilious vomiting + thick smooth muscle.
  • Hirschsprung = no ganglion cells.
  • Achalasia = bird beak + myenteric ganglion loss; dysphagia to solids and liquids.
  • GERD = basal hyperplasia + elongated papillae + eosinophils.
  • Varices = dilated distal submucosal veins → massive hematemesis.
  • Barrett = red velvety mucosa + goblet cells → adenocarcinoma risk.
  • PUD = punched-out clean crater; carcinoma = irregular invasive ulcer.
  • H. pylori = surface organisms + chronic-active gastritis + lymphoid follicles.
  • ZES = recurrent distal ulcers + gastrin ↑.
  • Diffuse gastric CA = linitis plastica + signet-ring cells.
  • Volvulus = twisting; intussusception = telescoping.
  • Celiac = villous atrophy + crypt hyperplasia + IELs.
  • Crohn = skip + cobblestone + transmural + fistula/stricture ± granuloma.
  • UC = continuous rectal disease + crypt abscesses + pseudopolyps; granulomas absent.
  • Adenoma = dysplastic but noninvasive; submucosal invasion = colorectal adenocarcinoma.
  • Appendicitis = neutrophils in muscularis propria.
  • Cirrhosis = regenerative nodules + bridging fibrosis.
  • PSC = onion-skin fibrosis.
  • Acute pancreatitis = chalky fat necrosis/saponification + lipase ↑.
  • Pancreatic adenocarcinoma = firm infiltrative mass + malignant glands + desmoplasia/perineural invasion.
  • Tumor-marker rule: CEA, AFP, and CA19-9 support surveillance/clinical correlation; none substitutes for demonstration of invasive malignant morphology.
Plate VI — Gastrointestinal DiseasesPathology Rapid Review · PLE Board
🫀VII
Regional block · heart failure, congenital heart disease, ischemic heart disease, hypertensive…

Cardiovascular and Collagen Disorders

10% of Pathology PLE

▪Heart Failure

Left-Sided Heart Failure

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossHeavy, wet, congested lungs ± cardiomegaly/LV dilation
MicroscopyPulmonary capillary congestion, alveolar edema, hemosiderin-laden “heart-failure cells”
Confirming stain/markerPrussian blue SUPPLEMENTARY highlights hemosiderin in alveolar macrophages
Mimic killerDiffuse alveolar hemorrhage → HF favors passive congestion + cardiac disease rather than capillaritis
LabBNP/NT-proBNP ↑ SUPPLEMENTARY with ventricular strain
Pathognomonic?Heart-failure cells are characteristic of prior pulmonary hemorrhage/congestion, not one specific cardiac cause
  • Robbins describes pulmonary edema as producing heavy wet lungs and identifies hemosiderin-laden macrophages as telltale heart-failure cells.

Right-Sided Heart Failure

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossNutmeg liver + hepatomegaly + dependent edema + ascites
MicroscopyCentral-vein/sinusoidal congestion ± centrilobular hepatocyte necrosis/fibrosis
Confirming stain/markerH&E; no disease-specific IHC
Mimic killerPrimary cirrhosis → right HF shows dominant centrilobular passive congestion with systemic venous findings
LabAST/ALT may ↑ with severe hepatic congestion/hypoperfusion
Pathognomonic?Nutmeg liver is characteristic, not unique to one cause of venous congestion
  • Rubin describes the alternating dark-red congested central zones and yellow peripheral hepatic zones that produce the classic “nutmeg liver.”

▪Congenital Heart Disease

Atrial Septal Defect — ASD

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossSeptal opening, usually secundum type, with RA/RV dilation
MicroscopyNo specific cellular lesion; chronic large shunts may cause pulmonary arterial medial hypertrophy
Confirming testEchocardiography
Mimic killerPFO → flap-like incomplete fusion; ASD = true tissue defect
LabNo serum marker
Pathognomonic?Anatomic septal defect is diagnostic

Ventricular Septal Defect — VSD

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossUsually membranous septal opening ± RV hypertrophy/pulmonary-artery enlargement
MicroscopySecondary pulmonary arterial medial hypertrophy/intimal lesions if large
Confirming testEchocardiography
Mimic killerASD → communication is ventricular and large VSDs cause earlier pulmonary pressure overload
LabNo specific lab
ComplicationPulmonary vascular remodeling → Eisenmenger syndrome
Pathognomonic?Anatomic ventricular communication is diagnostic

Patent Ductus Arteriosus — PDA

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossPersistent channel between descending aorta and pulmonary artery
MicroscopyNo unique lesion; secondary pulmonary vascular remodeling in large chronic shunts
Confirming testEchocardiography
Mimic killerAortopulmonary window → PDA has the characteristic ductal anatomic connection
LabNo serum marker
Classic clueContinuous “machinery” murmur SUPPLEMENTARY
ComplicationEisenmenger reversal may produce differential cyanosis

Eisenmenger Syndrome

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossLongstanding congenital shunt with RV hypertrophy and pulmonary arterial enlargement
MicroscopySevere pulmonary arterial medial hypertrophy/intimal occlusion ± advanced plexiform change SUPPLEMENTARY
Confirming testEcho/catheterization demonstrating reversed/bidirectional shunt
Mimic killerPrimary cyanotic CHD → Eisenmenger begins as a left-to-right shunt before reversal
LabSecondary erythrocytosis may develop
Pathognomonic?Shunt reversal from fixed pulmonary hypertension defines the syndrome

Tetralogy of Fallot

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossHIGHLY CHARACTERISTIC Boot-shaped heart with RVH, VSD, overriding aorta and RV outflow obstruction
MicroscopyMarked RV cardiomyocyte hypertrophy; no unique cellular lesion
Confirming testEchocardiography
Mimic killerEisenmenger → TOF has primary pulmonary outflow obstruction + overriding aorta
LabChronic hypoxemia → secondary erythrocytosis
Pathognomonic?Four-part anatomic constellation is diagnostic; boot shape alone is characteristic

Coarctation of the Aorta

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossSharp juxtaductal aortic narrowing + LV hypertrophy + enlarged collateral arteries
MicroscopyRidge contains smooth-muscle/elastic tissue; secondary LV myocyte hypertrophy
Confirming testImaging
Mimic killerAcquired aortic obstruction → congenital juxtaductal narrowing + collateral intercostal arteries
LabNo specific serum marker
Classic clueInferior rib notching from enlarged intercostal collateral vessels
Pathognomonic?Rib notching is characteristic, not unique

▪Ischemic Heart Disease

Stable Angina

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossCoronary atherosclerosis; myocardium often grossly normal
MicroscopyNo acute coagulative necrosis in uncomplicated transient ischemia
Confirming markerNo MI-pattern troponin elevation
Mimic killerMI → myocyte necrosis + troponin rise
LabTroponin typically not elevated from uncomplicated stable angina
Pathognomonic?No pathognomonic histology

Acute Myocardial Infarction

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossEvolves from no visible lesion → dark mottling → yellow-tan softening → gray-white scar
MicroscopyCoagulative necrosis → neutrophils → macrophages → granulation tissue → collagen scar
Confirming markerCardiac troponin I/T ↑
Mimic killerMyocarditis → inflammatory injury is not restricted to a coronary vascular territory
LabTroponin ↑ after irreversible myocyte injury
Pathognomonic?Temporal morphology is highly characteristic, but clinical/coronary correlation is required

MI Age — Must-Know Table

TimeGrossMicroscopic hallmark
0–4 hUsually noneNone ± wavy fibers
4–12 hOccasional dark mottlingEarly coagulative necrosis, edema, hemorrhage
12–24 hDark mottlingHypereosinophilic myocytes, pyknosis, early neutrophils
1–3 dMottled/yellow-tan centerBrisk neutrophils
3–7 dYellow-tan soft centerMacrophages begin phagocytosis
7–10 dMaximally softMacrophages + early granulation tissue
10–14 dRed-gray depressed marginsGranulation tissue
2–8 wkGray-whiteCollagen deposition ↑
>2 moFirm white scarDense collagenous scar
  • Robbins' infarct table shows exactly this sequence, including wavy fibers, neutrophils, macrophage clearance, granulation tissue, and mature scar.
  • Masson trichrome highlights collagen in healing and healed myocardial infarcts.
  • 3–7 days = rupture danger: macrophage digestion makes the infarcted wall mechanically weakest.

Sudden Cardiac Death — Ischemic

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossSevere CAD ± old myocardial scars; acute infarct may be invisible
MicroscopyMay show no acute LM change if death occurs very early; old scars show dense fibrosis
Confirming markerNo specific pathology marker for fatal arrhythmia
Mimic killerChannelopathy → usually structurally normal heart versus CAD/scar substrate
LabTroponin may be normal/minimally elevated if death is very early
Pathognomonic?None

▪Hypertensive Disease

Systemic Hypertensive Heart Disease

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossConcentric LV hypertrophy, initially without chamber dilation
MicroscopyHypertrophied myocytes with enlarged nuclei ± interstitial fibrosis
Confirming stain/markerH&E; trichrome SUPPLEMENTARY highlights fibrosis
Mimic killerHCM → hypertensive LVH lacks extensive myofiber disarray
Lab/testHypertension + echo evidence of LVH
Pathognomonic?Concentric LVH is characteristic, not specific for hypertension

Cor Pulmonale

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossAcute = RV dilation without hypertrophy; chronic = RV hypertrophy ± dilation
MicroscopyChronic RV cardiomyocyte hypertrophy ± fibrosis
Confirming testEcho + evidence of pulmonary/pulmonary-vascular disease
Mimic killerRight HF secondary to LV failure → true cor pulmonale has primary pulmonary cause
LabNo specific cardiac serum marker
Pathognomonic?RV morphology alone is not specific; pulmonary cause is required

▪Valvular Heart Disease

Mitral Valve Prolapse

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossFloppy, redundant, thick, rubbery leaflets with ballooning/“hooding” into LA
MicroscopyMyxomatous degeneration of spongiosa + attenuation of fibrosa
Confirming test/stainEchocardiography; Alcian blue SUPPLEMENTARY highlights mucopolysaccharide-rich matrix
Mimic killerChronic RHD → MVP has no commissural fusion
LabNo serum marker
Pathognomonic?Myxomatous change is characteristic but can occur secondary to other regurgitant lesions
  • Robbins identifies ballooning/hooding and myxomatous expansion of the spongiosa as the characteristic anatomic and microscopic lesions of MVP.

Acute Rheumatic Fever

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossTiny sterile verrucae along lines of valve closure ± fibrinous pericarditis
MicroscopyAschoff bodies containing Anitschkow “caterpillar” cells
Confirming testEvidence of preceding GAS infection: ASO/anti-DNase B SUPPLEMENTARY
Mimic killerIE → rheumatic verrucae are small, sterile and closure-line based, not large/destructive
LabEvidence of preceding streptococcal infection
Pathognomonic?Aschoff bodies with characteristic Anitschkow cells are very highly characteristic of rheumatic carditis
  • Robbins defines Aschoff bodies and the central wavy chromatin of Anitschkow “caterpillar” cells and illustrates the small closure-line verrucae.

Chronic Rheumatic Heart Disease

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossThick fibrotic valve with commissural fusion + shortened/fused chordae → fish-mouth/buttonhole stenosis SUPPLEMENTARY
MicroscopyDense valvular fibrosis ± neovascularization/calcification
Confirming stain/markerH&E; trichrome SUPPLEMENTARY highlights scar
Mimic killerDegenerative calcific disease → commissural fusion and chordal fusion strongly favor RHD
LabNo active streptococcal organism in chronic valve lesion
Pathognomonic?Commissural/chordal fusion is highly characteristic
  • Robbins illustrates diffuse fibrous leaflet thickening, commissural fusion, and thickened/fused chordae in chronic RHD.

Infective Endocarditis

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossLarge, bulky, friable, irregular vegetations ± cusp perforation/ring abscess
MicroscopyFibrin/platelets + inflammatory debris + microbial colonies ± valve destruction
Confirming marker/testBlood cultures + echocardiography; Gram/organism-specific stain
Mimic killerNBTE/Libman-Sacks → IE has microorganisms + active destruction/inflammation
LabPositive blood cultures; inflammatory markers may ↑
Pathognomonic?Organisms in a destructive vegetation are diagnostic

▪Pericardial Disease

Fibrinous Pericarditis

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossShaggy rough fibrin coating → “bread-and-butter” pericarditis SUPPLEMENTARY
MicroscopyEosinophilic fibrin over inflamed pericardial surface
Confirming stain/markerH&E
Mimic killerPurulent pericarditis → abundant neutrophilic pus/microorganisms
LabEtiology-dependent; no universal serum marker
Pathognomonic?Bread-and-butter appearance is characteristic of fibrinous pericarditis, not cause-specific

Pericardial Effusion / Hemopericardium / Cardiac Tamponade

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossTense pericardial sac containing serous fluid or blood
MicroscopyOften little intrinsic lesion; examine for inflammation, malignancy, or myocardial/aortic rupture
Confirming testEchocardiography
Mimic killerConstrictive pericarditis → tamponade = fluid-pressure compression, constriction = fibrotic shell
LabHemoglobin may fall with major bleeding; no specific tamponade marker
Pathognomonic?Hemodynamic chamber compression establishes tamponade clinically/imaging-wise

Constrictive Pericarditis

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossDense fibrotic/fibrocalcific shell encasing heart → concretio cordis
MicroscopyDense collagen ± dystrophic calcification
Confirming stain/testImaging; trichrome SUPPLEMENTARY highlights collagen
Mimic killerRestrictive cardiomyopathy → constriction has abnormal thick pericardium, restriction has intrinsic myocardium
LabNo specific marker
Pathognomonic?Concretio cordis is highly characteristic of severe constrictive pericarditis

▪Cardiomyopathies

Dilated Cardiomyopathy — DCM

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossEnlarged, heavy, flabby heart with four-chamber dilation ± mural thrombi
MicroscopyNonspecific myocyte hypertrophy/stretching + interstitial/endocardial fibrosis
Confirming stain/markerMasson trichrome highlights interstitial fibrosis
Mimic killerIschemic cardiomyopathy → DCM lacks enough CAD/infarct scarring to explain global dysfunction
LabBNP may ↑; EF markedly ↓ in advanced disease
Pathognomonic?Gross phenotype is characteristic; histology is explicitly nonspecific
  • Robbins describes DCM as an enlarged, heavy, flabby four-chamber dilated heart, frequently with mural thrombi and nonspecific interstitial fibrosis.

Hypertrophic Cardiomyopathy — HCM

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossAsymmetric septal hypertrophy, small LV cavity, often “banana-like” configuration
MicroscopyMassive myocyte hypertrophy + myofiber disarray + interstitial fibrosis
Confirming test/stainEcho; H&E demonstrates disarray; trichrome highlights fibrosis
Mimic killerHypertensive LVH → extensive haphazard myofiber disarray favors HCM
LabNo disease-specific serum marker
Pathognomonic?Myofiber disarray is highly characteristic but not absolutely unique
  • Robbins identifies asymmetric septal hypertrophy, banana-like LV cavity, and haphazard myofiber disarray as the classic morphologic pattern.

Restrictive Cardiomyopathy — Cardiac Amyloidosis

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossFirm, thickened/rubbery heart ± normal/small ventricular cavities
MicroscopyExtracellular amorphous eosinophilic amyloid deposits separating cardiomyocytes and involving vessels
Confirming stain/markerCongo red → apple-green birefringence under polarized light DIAGNOSTIC
Mimic killerHCM → amyloid has extracellular Congo-red-positive deposits rather than myofiber disarray
LabDepends on amyloid type; no universal “cardiac amyloid” serum marker
Pathognomonic?Congo red + apple-green birefringence confirms amyloid, not its biochemical subtype
  • Robbins explicitly describes amorphous extracellular cardiac amyloid and its Congo-red positivity with apple-green birefringence.

Cardiomyopathy 10-Second Table

FeatureDCMHCMAmyloid RCM
ChamberDilatedSmall LV cavityUsually nondilated ventricles
WallVariable hypertrophyVery thick septumThick/stiff
DysfunctionSystolicDiastolic ± obstructionDiastolic/restrictive
Signature microNonspecific fibrosisMyofiber disarrayAmyloid
Signature stainTrichromeH&ECongo red
Gross buzzwordFlabbyBanana LVFirm/rubbery

▪Primary Cardiac Tumors

Atrial Myxoma

Rapid-review elementFinding
BehaviorBENIGN
GrossGelatinous sessile/pedunculated atrial mass, classically near fossa ovalis, usually left atrium
MicroscopyScattered stellate/polygonal myxoma cells in abundant myxoid matrix, sometimes arranged around vessels
Confirming stain/markerClassic H&E morphology; no required serum tumor marker
Mimic killerOrganized thrombus → thrombus lacks true lesional myxoma cells in myxoid stroma
Lab/tumor markerNo specific serum tumor marker
SpreadEmbolization, not metastasis
  • Robbins describes myxomas as benign tumors usually arising in the atria near the fossa ovalis, ranging from firm hemorrhagic masses to soft gelatinous lesions.

Cardiac Rhabdomyoma

Rapid-review elementFinding
BehaviorBENIGN / hamartoma-like
GrossMultiple pale intramyocardial nodules, usually in children
MicroscopyGlycogen-rich cells with radiating cytoplasmic strands → “spider cells”
Confirming stain/markerPAS SUPPLEMENTARY highlights glycogen
Mimic killerFibroma → collagen-rich tumor lacks spider cells
Lab/tumor markerNone
AssociationTuberous sclerosis
MetastasisNone

Papillary Fibroelastoma

Rapid-review elementFinding
BehaviorBENIGN
GrossSmall mobile valvular “sea-anemone” lesion
MicroscopyPapillary fronds with collagen/elastic cores covered by endothelium
Confirming stain/markerElastic stain SUPPLEMENTARY may highlight cores
Mimic killerLambl excrescence → fibroelastoma is larger and mass-forming
Lab/tumor markerNone
SpreadEmbolization, not metastasis
  • Robbins calls papillary fibroelastomas sea-anemone–shaped benign endocardial tumors and distinguishes them from much smaller Lambl excrescences.

Primary Cardiac Angiosarcoma

Rapid-review elementFinding
BehaviorMALIGNANT
GrossInfiltrative hemorrhagic cardiac mass, commonly right-sided SUPPLEMENTARY
MicroscopyMalignant endothelial cells forming irregular vascular channels
Confirming markerCD31, ERG SUPPLEMENTARY
Mimic killerBenign vascular lesion → angiosarcoma shows infiltration, cytologic malignancy and destructive growth
Tumor markerNo serum marker
Malignant criterionInvasion and hematogenous metastasis
  • Robbins emphasizes that the common primary cardiac tumors are overwhelmingly benign and that malignant primary cardiac tumors are rare, most often sarcomas such as angiosarcoma.

▪Collagen / Collagen-Vascular Disorders

SLE — Libman-Sacks Endocarditis

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossSmall sterile verrucous vegetations on either/both surfaces of valve leaflets
MicroscopySterile fibrinous/fibrinoid valvular lesions ± inflammation
Confirming markerSLE serology: ANA, anti-dsDNA, complement pattern
Mimic killerIE → no organisms and little destructive suppuration in Libman-Sacks
LabANA/anti-dsDNA +; complement may ↓ during active SLE
Pathognomonic?Libman-Sacks is highly characteristic of SLE, not a microbial vegetation

Rheumatoid Arthritis — Cardiac Rheumatoid Nodule

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossSmall nodular lesion ± fibrinous pericarditis
MicroscopyCentral fibrinoid necrosis surrounded by palisading macrophages
Confirming markerRF/anti-CCP support systemic RA
Mimic killerRheumatic Aschoff body → RA has palisading necrobiotic nodule, not Anitschkow cells
LabRF/anti-CCP may be positive
Pathognomonic?Palisading rheumatoid morphology is characteristic, not exclusive

Systemic Sclerosis — Cardiac Involvement

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossPatchy pale myocardial scars ± secondary chamber enlargement
MicroscopySmall-vessel intimal sclerosis + patchy interstitial/replacement myocardial fibrosis
Confirming stain/markerTrichrome SUPPLEMENTARY highlights fibrosis; systemic-sclerosis serology supports context
Mimic killerCAD scar → systemic sclerosis can show patchy microvascular fibrosis without corresponding major epicardial coronary obstruction
LabAnti-Scl-70/anticentromere support systemic phenotype
Pathognomonic?Cardiac fibrosis is nonspecific

Polyarteritis Nodosa — Cardiac Disease

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossMyocardial infarcts/scars ± hypertrophy from renovascular hypertension
MicroscopyTransmural necrotizing medium-artery vasculitis with fibrinoid necrosis ± thrombosis
Confirming stain/markerH&E; no single specific serum marker
Mimic killerAtherosclerotic MI → PAN shows active inflammatory destruction of coronary arterial wall
LabNonspecific inflammatory abnormalities
Pathognomonic?Necrotizing arteritis is characteristic of vasculitis but not PAN-specific

▪Vegetation Rapid Comparison

LesionGross location/appearanceMicroBest discriminator
Acute rheumatic feverTiny verrucae on closure linesFibrinoid injury; Aschoff bodies in heartSterile + closure line
Chronic RHDFibrotic fused valveDense fibrosisCommissural fusion
Infective endocarditisLarge friable destructive vegetationOrganisms + fibrin + inflammationMicrobial colonies/destruction
NBTESmall bland closure-line vegetationsPlatelet-fibrin thrombiNo organisms/inflammation
Libman-SacksSmall vegetations on either/both valve surfacesSterile fibrinoid/thrombotic lesionSLE context

▪Benign vs Malignant — Cardiac Tumor Rules

CriterionBenign cardiac tumorMalignant cardiac tumor
Local behaviorUsually circumscribedInfiltrative/destructive
CytologyBlandAtypia/mitoses may be prominent
MetastasisAbsentMay occur
MyxomaBenign—
RhabdomyomaBenign—
Papillary fibroelastomaBenign—
Angiosarcoma—Malignant
Clinical dangerObstruction, arrhythmia, embolizationSame + invasion/metastasis
Definitive malignant behavior—Metastasis or destructive invasion
Board trap: myxoma embolization ≠ metastasis.
Board trap: benign cardiac tumors can be fatal through valve obstruction, arrhythmia, or embolic stroke.
  • Robbins states that the five most common primary cardiac tumors have no malignant potential and constitute nearly 90% of primary cardiac tumors.
