▪Final 20-Second Board Recall

  • Left HF: heavy wet lungs + heart-failure cells.
  • Right HF: nutmeg liver.
  • ASD/VSD/PDA: initially left → right; late irreversible pulmonary hypertension can produce Eisenmenger reversal.
  • TOF: VSD + RV outflow obstruction + overriding aorta + RVH → boot-shaped heart.
  • Coarctation: upper-body hypertension + weak femoral pulses + rib notching.
  • Angina: ischemia without necrosis; MI = necrosis + troponin rise.
  • MI: 1–3 d neutrophils; 3–7 d macrophages + rupture risk; weeks → collagen scar.
  • Hypertension: concentric LVH.
  • Cor pulmonale: acute = RV dilation; chronic = RV hypertrophy.
  • MVP: floppy/hooded valve + myxomatous spongiosa.
  • Acute rheumatic fever: Aschoff body + Anitschkow caterpillar cells + closure-line verrucae.
  • Chronic RHD: commissural fusion + shortened fused chordae → fish-mouth stenosis.
  • IE: large friable destructive vegetation + organisms.
  • Fibrinous pericarditis: bread-and-butter.
  • Constrictive pericarditis: concretio cordis.
  • DCM: flabby, four-chamber dilated heart + mural thrombi + nonspecific fibrosis.
  • HCM: asymmetric septal hypertrophy + banana LV + myofiber disarray.
  • Amyloid RCM: Congo red + apple-green birefringence.
  • Adult primary cardiac tumor: myxoma → gelatinous fossa-ovalis mass.
  • Childhood primary cardiac tumor: rhabdomyoma → spider cells.
  • Papillary fibroelastoma: sea-anemone valve lesion.
  • SLE: Libman-Sacks sterile vegetations; RA: palisading rheumatoid nodules; systemic sclerosis: patchy myocardial fibrosis; PAN: necrotizing coronary arteritis.
  • Tumor rule: embolization by a benign myxoma is not metastasis; destructive invasion or metastasis defines malignant cardiac tumor behavior.s
Plate VII — Cardiovascular and Collagen DisordersPathology Rapid Review · PLE Board
🫁VIII
Regional block · Official TOS: 10% = 10 items covering atelectasis, pulmonary edema…

Respiratory Diseases

10% of Pathology PLE

▪Atelectasis / Alveolar Injury

Atelectasis

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossReduced-volume, dark-red, airless collapsed lung; resorption collapse pulls mediastinum toward, compression pushes it away
MicroscopyClosely apposed/folded alveolar septa with markedly reduced airspaces
Confirming stain/markerN/A; imaging demonstrates volume loss
Mimic killerPneumonia fills alveoli with inflammatory exudate but usually does not produce equivalent volume loss
LabHypoxemia may occur; no specific serum marker
Pathognomonic?Collapsed architecture + volume loss is a diagnostic pattern, but the cause must still be determined
  • Rubin specifically distinguishes obstructive atelectasis with mediastinal shift toward the collapsed lung from severe compression atelectasis, which can shift the mediastinum away.

Cardiogenic Pulmonary Edema

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossHeavy, wet, boggy lungs; chronic disease may become brown and firm
MicroscopyCongested alveolar septal capillaries + pale intra-alveolar edema ± hemosiderin-laden heart-failure cells
Confirming stain/markerPrussian blue SUPPLEMENTARY highlights hemosiderin in macrophages
Mimic killerARDS → hyaline membranes and epithelial necrosis rather than predominantly passive congestion
LabBNP/NT-proBNP ↑ SUPPLEMENTARY if cardiac failure is the cause
Pathognomonic?Heart-failure cells are characteristic of chronic pulmonary hemorrhage/congestion, not a single cardiac disorder

ARDS / Diffuse Alveolar Damage

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossHeavy, firm, red, boggy lungs, diffusely involved
MicroscopyHyaline membranes lining alveolar walls + edema + epithelial necrosis; later type II pneumocyte hyperplasia/fibrosis
Confirming stain/markerH&E demonstrates DAD; no etiologic IHC marker
Mimic killerCardiogenic edema → lacks the characteristic diffuse epithelial injury/hyaline membranes
LabSevere hypoxemia; PaO₂/FiO₂ ↓ SUPPLEMENTARY
Pathognomonic?Hyaline membranes are highly characteristic of DAD, but not uniquely specific to one cause of ARDS
  • Robbins describes acute DAD as heavy, firm red lungs with edema and waxy hyaline membranes composed of fibrin-rich edema fluid and necrotic epithelial debris; later lesions show type II pneumocyte proliferation and possible fibrosis.

▪Obstructive Pulmonary Disease

Emphysema

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossVoluminous, pale, overinflated lungs; bullae may occur
MicroscopyPermanent enlarged distal airspaces with destruction/thinning of alveolar septa
Confirming stain/markerH&E; serum α1-antitrypsin level SUPPLEMENTARY if deficiency suspected
Mimic killerCompensatory hyperinflation → enlarged airspaces but alveolar walls remain intact
LabFEV1/FVC ↓; DLCO commonly ↓ SUPPLEMENTARY
Pathognomonic?Alveolar-wall destruction is the defining morphologic criterion

Emphysema Pattern Discriminator

TypeDistributionBoard clue
CentriacinarCentral/proximal acinus, classically upper lobesSmoking
PanacinarEntire acinus uniformly enlarged, classically lower zonesα1-antitrypsin deficiency
ParaseptalDistal acinus/subpleuralBullae → spontaneous pneumothorax
  • Rubin's figures contrast central respiratory-bronchiolar destruction in centrilobular disease with uniform whole-acinus damage in panacinar emphysema.

Chronic Bronchitis

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossHyperemic, edematous bronchi filled with excessive mucous/mucopurulent secretions
MicroscopyEnlarged submucosal mucus glands + goblet-cell hyperplasia + chronic airway inflammation/fibrosis
Confirming stain/markerReid index ↑; H&E
Mimic killerEmphysema → chronic bronchitis is dominated by airway mucus-gland disease, not alveolar-wall destruction
LabObstructive PFT: FEV1/FVC ↓
Pathognomonic?Increased Reid index is characteristic, not independently pathognomonic
  • Robbins identifies enlargement of mucus-secreting glands as the most striking change and states that the Reid index, normally about 0.4, increases in chronic bronchitis.

Bronchial Asthma

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossOverinflated lungs with thick tenacious mucus plugs obstructing bronchi/bronchioles
MicroscopyEosinophils + sub-basement membrane fibrosis + smooth-muscle hypertrophy/hyperplasia + goblet/gland hyperplasia
Classic materialCurschmann spirals and Charcot-Leyden crystals in mucus/sputum
Confirming stain/markerH&E; eosinophils/IgE may support atopic disease SUPPLEMENTARY
Mimic killerChronic bronchitis → asthma favors eosinophils + spirals/crystals + striking smooth-muscle hypertrophy
LabReversible airflow obstruction; eosinophilia/IgE may ↑
Pathognomonic?Curschmann spirals and Charcot-Leyden crystals are characteristic, not specific
  • Robbins describes mucus plugging, eosinophils, Curschmann spirals, Charcot-Leyden crystals, sub-basement membrane fibrosis, and smooth-muscle hypertrophy as characteristic asthma morphology.

Bronchiectasis

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossCystically dilated bronchi filled with mucopurulent secretions, often traceable almost to pleural surface
MicroscopyAcute/chronic airway inflammation with ulceration and destruction of smooth muscle and elastic wall ± fibrosis
Confirming stain/markerN/A; CT confirms permanent bronchial dilation
Mimic killerChronic bronchitis → bronchiectasis requires permanent structural airway dilation
LabSputum cultures during infection/exacerbation
Pathognomonic?Permanent bronchial dilation from wall destruction is the defining morphologic lesion
  • Robbins notes that bronchi may be dilated up to fourfold and can be followed nearly to the pleural surface; Rubin likewise emphasizes cystic, mucopurulent, permanently dilated bronchi.

▪Restrictive / Interstitial Disease

Usual Interstitial Pneumonia / Idiopathic Pulmonary Fibrosis

[supplementary — Representative Morphology for the TOS Category “restrictive Pulmonary disease”]

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossFirm, shrunken lower/subpleural lungs with honeycomb change
MicroscopyPatchy temporally heterogeneous fibrosis + fibroblastic foci + remodeled cystic spaces
Confirming stain/markerH&E; trichrome may highlight collagen SUPPLEMENTARY
Mimic killerNSIP → uniform/temporally homogeneous fibrosis rather than patchwork old and young lesions
LabRestrictive PFT: TLC/FVC ↓, FEV1/FVC preserved or ↑
Pathognomonic?UIP pattern is highly characteristic, but secondary causes must be excluded before calling disease idiopathic

▪Pneumoconioses

Coal Workers’ Pneumoconiosis

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossBlack upper-zone macules/nodules; severe disease → large black progressive massive fibrosis scars
MicroscopyCarbon-laden macrophages around respiratory bronchioles; nodules add delicate collagen
Confirming stain/markerH&E/polarization as needed; no specific serum test
Mimic killerSimple anthracosis → lacks significant fibrotic nodules/progressive massive fibrosis
LabRestrictive/obstructive impairment only in more advanced disease
Pathognomonic?Carbon pigment alone = exposure; not diagnostic of clinically significant CWP
  • Robbins describes coal macules as 1–2 mm collections of carbon-laden macrophages, with nodules adding collagen and a predominantly upper-zone distribution.

Silicosis

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossUpper-lobe pale/black fibrotic nodules ± coalescent scars; hilar nodes may show “eggshell” calcification
MicroscopyConcentrically whorled/hyalinized collagen nodules with dust-laden macrophages
Confirming stain/markerPolarized light demonstrates weakly birefringent particles
Mimic killerSarcoidosis → silicosis has hyalinized whorled nodules + mineral particles, not naked granulomas
LabN/A; TB testing important because susceptibility is increased
Pathognomonic?Whorled nodules are characteristic, not absolutely exclusive

Asbestosis

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossLower-lobe/subpleural interstitial fibrosis ± well-demarcated calcified pleural plaques
MicroscopyGolden-brown beaded/fusiform asbestos bodies within fibrotic lung
Confirming stain/markerPrussian blue highlights the iron-protein coating
Mimic killerIPF → asbestos exposure is supported by asbestos bodies + characteristic distribution/pleural plaques
Lab/tumor associationNo serum marker; increased risk of lung carcinoma and mesothelioma
Pathognomonic?An asbestos body shows exposure; asbestos bodies alone do not establish asbestosis without interstitial fibrosis
  • Robbins describes asbestos bodies as iron-coated structures and emphasizes that mineral-dust disease morphology must be interpreted with fibrosis and exposure.

▪Sarcoidosis

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossBilateral hilar/mediastinal lymphadenopathy ± multiple pale noncavitating pulmonary nodules
MicroscopyTight noncaseating epithelioid granulomas with giant cells and relatively little peripheral inflammation
Classic inclusionsAsteroid bodies / Schaumann bodies may occur
Confirming stain/markerAFB and fungal stains negative after exclusion of infection; biopsy demonstrates granulomas
Mimic killerTB → sarcoid favors noncaseating “naked” granulomas with negative organism studies
LabACE may ↑; urine/serum calcium may ↑, but neither is specific
Pathognomonic?Neither noncaseating granulomas nor asteroid/Schaumann bodies are pathognomonic
  • Rubin explicitly states that asteroid and Schaumann bodies are not specific, and that no laboratory test is specific for sarcoidosis; ACE elevation occurs only in a proportion of active cases.

▪Pulmonary Vascular Disease

Pulmonary Thromboembolism / Pulmonary Infarction

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossLarge embolus may form a saddle embolus; infarct = peripheral wedge-shaped red hemorrhagic lesion, base against pleura
MicroscopyHemorrhagic ischemic necrosis of alveolar septa + extensive intra-alveolar RBCs
Confirming stain/markerH&E; Lines of Zahn support antemortem thrombus
Mimic killerLobar pneumonia → infarction is peripheral wedge shaped with vascular occlusion, not lobar neutrophilic consolidation
LabD-dimer has rule-out utility; CT angiography demonstrates embolus SUPPLEMENTARY
Pathognomonic?A peripheral hemorrhagic wedge is highly characteristic, not by itself proof of embolic origin
  • Robbins emphasizes that lung infarcts are usually hemorrhagic because of the organ's dual blood supply and tend to be wedge-shaped with the occluded vessel at the apex.

Diffuse Pulmonary Hemorrhage — Anti-GBM / Goodpasture Prototype

[supplementary Entity for the TOS Category “pulmonary hemorrhage”]

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossHeavy, dark-red, blood-filled lungs; chronic disease may become brown from hemosiderin
MicroscopyDiffuse intra-alveolar RBCs + hemosiderin-laden macrophages
Confirming stain/markerPrussian blue for siderophages; linear IgG/C3 on IF CONFIRMATORY
Mimic killerImmune-complex pulmonary hemorrhage → granular rather than linear IF
LabAnti-GBM antibodies; anemia ± renal dysfunction
Pathognomonic?Linear anti-GBM IF is diagnostic of anti-GBM disease in the proper setting

▪Pulmonary Infections

Community-Acquired Bacterial Pneumonia — Lobar Pattern

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossUniform lobar consolidation progressing through red hepatization → gray hepatization
MicroscopyRed phase: alveoli packed with RBCs + neutrophils + fibrin; gray phase: fibrinosuppurative exudate with RBC breakdown
Confirming stain/markerGram stain/culture
Mimic killerViral pneumonia → predominantly interstitial mononuclear inflammation, not alveolar neutrophilic exudate
LabNeutrophilic leukocytosis; cultures may identify organism
Pathognomonic?Hepatization is characteristic of lobar bacterial pneumonia, not organism-specific
  • Rubin describes the firm liver-like lung of red hepatization, followed after neutrophil/RBC breakdown by gray hepatization.

Bronchopneumonia Pattern

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossPatchy, multilobar, often bilateral/basal gray-red/yellow consolidation
MicroscopyBronchioles and surrounding alveoli filled with neutrophils
Confirming stain/markerGram stain/culture
Mimic killerLobar pneumonia → bronchopneumonia is patchy and airway centered
LabLeukocytosis/culture according to organism
Pathognomonic?Pattern is characteristic, not organism-specific

Hospital-Acquired Pneumonia

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossUsually patchy bronchopneumonic consolidation ± necrosis/abscess
MicroscopyNeutrophil-rich bronchiolar/alveolar exudate ± necrotizing parenchymal injury
Confirming stain/markerGram stain + respiratory/blood cultures
Mimic killerCAP → histology may be identical; hospital/device exposure and microbiology distinguish HAP
LabCulture identifies resistant gram-negative organisms/MRSA as applicable
Pathognomonic?No HAP-specific histologic lesion

Aspiration Pneumonia

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossPatchy dependent-lung consolidation ± necrosis/abscess
MicroscopyAcute epithelial necrosis/neutrophils; chronic aspiration shows foreign material with foreign-body giant cells/granulomas
Confirming stain/markerH&E demonstrates aspirated material; culture if secondarily infected
Mimic killerSarcoidosis → aspiration granulomas contain visible foreign material and foreign-body giant-cell reaction
LabNo specific serum marker
Pathognomonic?Demonstrable aspirated material is strongly diagnostic; distribution alone is not

Lung Abscess

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossIrregular pus-filled necrotic cavity; chronic severe lesions may become fetid/multiloculated
MicroscopySuppurative liquefactive destruction with neutrophils and necrotic debris; chronic wall develops granulation tissue/fibrosis
Confirming stain/markerGram/culture ± fungal/AFB stains according to context
Mimic killerCavitating carcinoma → viable malignant cells in cavity wall favor cancer
LabNeutrophilic leukocytosis; culture according to cause
Pathognomonic?Suppurative cavity is characteristic, not etiologically specific

Chronic Granulomatous Pneumonia — Histoplasmosis Prototype

[supplementary Representative of “chronic pneumonia”]

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossNecrotizing granulomatous nodules ± cavitation; healed foci may fibrose/calcify
MicroscopyNecrotizing granulomas; small yeast forms may be intracellular in macrophages
Confirming stain/markerGMS/PAS highlights fungal organisms
Mimic killerTB → Histoplasma is GMS/PAS positive, whereas TB is acid-fast
LabFungal antigen/culture as applicable
Pathognomonic?Granuloma morphology alone is not specific; organism identification is confirmatory

▪Pulmonary Tumors

Lung Adenocarcinoma

Rapid-review elementFinding
BehaviorMALIGNANT
GrossUsually peripheral firm gray-white mass ± pleural puckering
MicroscopyMalignant gland formation and/or mucin; some tumors show lepidic growth along alveolar septa
Confirming markerTTF-1, Napsin A support pulmonary adenocarcinoma
Mimic killerMetastatic adenocarcinoma → pulmonary morphology + TTF-1/Napsin A panel supports lung primary, but no single marker is absolute
Tumor marker/labNo reliable serum diagnostic tumor marker
Malignant criterionStromal invasion, lymphovascular spread, or metastasis
  • Robbins illustrates gland-forming lung adenocarcinoma with TTF-1 expression and identifies TTF-1/Napsin A as adenocarcinoma-associated markers.

Squamous Cell Carcinoma of Lung

Rapid-review elementFinding
BehaviorMALIGNANT
GrossOften central/hilar, firm gray-white endobronchial mass; cavitation is common
MicroscopyKeratin pearls + individual keratinization ± intercellular bridges
Confirming markerp40/p63 supports squamous differentiation
Mimic killerAdenocarcinoma → SCC shows squamous keratinization/bridges, not gland/mucin formation
Tumor marker/labPTHrP → Ca²⁺ ↑ in paraneoplastic hypercalcemia
Malignant criterionInvasion/metastasis
  • Rubin describes central gray-white ulcerated squamous tumors with frequent cavitation and classic keratin pearls/intercellular bridges.
  • Robbins associates lung squamous carcinoma with PTHrP-related hypercalcemia.

Small Cell Lung Carcinoma

Rapid-review elementFinding
BehaviorHIGH-GRADE MALIGNANT / early metastatic
GrossOften central with bulky hilar/mediastinal disease and extensive necrosis
MicroscopySmall blue cells with scant cytoplasm, nuclear molding, salt-and-pepper chromatin, high mitotic rate and necrosis ± Azzopardi effect
Confirming markerSynaptophysin, chromogranin, CD56
Mimic killerLymphoma → small cell favors nuclear molding + epithelial/neuroendocrine phenotype + extensive necrosis
Tumor marker/labSIADH → Na⁺ ↓; ectopic ACTH → cortisol ↑
Malignant criterionEarly widespread nodal/hematogenous metastasis
  • Robbins describes prominent nuclear molding, finely granular chromatin, high mitotic activity, extensive necrosis and neuroendocrine-marker expression in small cell carcinoma.
  • Small cell lung carcinoma is a major source of ectopic ADH and ACTH syndromes.

Three-Way Lung Carcinoma Discriminator

FeatureAdenocarcinomaSquamous carcinomaSmall cell carcinoma
Typical locationPeripheralCentralOften central
HallmarkGlands/mucinKeratin pearls/bridgesNuclear molding
MarkerTTF-1, Napsin Ap40, p63Synaptophysin/chromogranin/CD56
Classic paraneoplasticN/APTHrP → hypercalcemiaSIADH / ACTH
BehaviorMalignantMalignantHighly malignant / early spread

▪Pleural Disease

Pleural Effusion

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossClear straw fluid = hydrothorax; pus = empyema; blood = hemothorax; milky fluid = chylothorax
MicroscopyTransudate has little inflammation; empyema has dense neutrophils; malignant effusion may contain tumor cells
Confirming testThoracentesis/fluid analysis; cytology when malignancy suspected
Mimic killerPulmonary edema → effusion is outside the lung in pleural cavity, edema is intra-alveolar/interstitial
LabPleural protein/LDH studies SUPPLEMENTARY separate exudate from transudate
Pathognomonic?No single effusion appearance defines etiology

Pneumothorax

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossCollapsed lung retracted toward hilum with air in pleural cavity; apical subpleural blebs may be visible
MicroscopyPneumothorax itself has no unique microscopic lesion; underlying bleb/emphysema may be evident
Confirming testImaging
Mimic killerPleural effusion → both compress lung, but pneumothorax contains air rather than fluid
LabN/A
Pathognomonic?Pleural air with lung collapse is anatomically diagnostic

Malignant Mesothelioma

Rapid-review elementFinding
BehaviorMALIGNANT
GrossDiffuse thick pleural rind encasing/ensheathing the lung ± large pleural effusion
MicroscopyEpithelioid, sarcomatoid, or biphasic tumor; epithelioid type often tubulopapillary
Confirming markersCalretinin, WT1, CK5/6, podoplanin positive; Claudin-4 negative favors mesothelioma over adenocarcinoma
Mimic killerPleural metastatic adenocarcinoma → Claudin-4 positive / mesothelial-marker profile absent
Tumor marker/labNo serum marker replaces biopsy/IHC
Malignant criterionInvasion of pleural fat/lung/chest wall ± metastasis
  • Robbins describes mesothelioma as a diffuse pleural tumor that ensheathes the lung, with epithelioid, sarcomatoid, and biphasic patterns; calretinin, WT1, CK5/6 and podoplanin support mesothelial lineage, while Claudin-4 negativity helps distinguish adenocarcinoma.

▪Benign vs Malignant — Respiratory Tumor Safety

CriterionBenign / preinvasiveMalignant
Basement membranePreservedBreached
Stromal invasionAbsentPresent
Vascular/lymphatic invasionAbsentStrong evidence of malignancy
MetastasisAbsentDefinitive malignant behavior
Adenocarcinoma in situLepidic growth without invasionInvasive adenocarcinoma penetrates stroma
Squamous CISFull-thickness atypia but no invasionInvasive SCC crosses basement membrane
Pleural plaqueBenign/nonneoplastic exposure markerMesothelioma invades pleura/chest wall
Tumor IHCEstablishes lineageDoes not by itself prove malignant behavior
Tumor markerMay be abnormal or normalNever substitutes for morphology/invasion
  • Robbins explicitly defines carcinoma in situ as not penetrating the basement membrane, in contrast with invasive squamous carcinoma.

▪Pathognomonic vs Characteristic — Board Safety

Lung volume lossDEFININGAtelectasis
Heart-failure cellsCHARACTERISTICChronic pulmonary venous congestion
Hyaline membranesHIGHLY CHARACTERISTICDAD/ARDS; not etiologically specific
Alveolar-wall destructionDEFININGEmphysema
Reid index ↑CHARACTERISTICChronic bronchitis
Curschmann spiralsCHARACTERISTICAsthma but not exclusive
Charcot-Leyden crystalsSUGGESTIVEEosinophilic disease, not asthma-specific

▪One-Look Rapid Grid

EntityGross → MicroConfirm / labSingle mimic killer
AtelectasisSmall airless lung → collapsed alveoliImagingVolume loss
Pulmonary edemaHeavy wet lung → alveolar fluid/heart-failure cellsPrussian blue; BNP SUPPPassive congestion
ARDSHeavy red firm lung → hyaline membranesClinical oxygenation + H&EDAD
EmphysemaOverinflated pale lung → septal destructionPFTActual alveolar-wall destruction
Chronic bronchitisMucus-filled bronchi → mucous gland enlargementReid index ↑Airway gland disease
AsthmaMucus plugs → eosinophils/spirals/crystalsReversible PFTSmooth-muscle hypertrophy/eosinophils
BronchiectasisCystic pus-filled bronchi → wall destructionCTPermanent dilation
UIP/IPFHoneycomb basal lung → patchy fibrosis/fibroblastic fociPFT/biopsyTemporal heterogeneity
CWPBlack nodules → carbon macrophagesExposureFibrosis distinguishes from anthracosis
SilicosisUpper nodules → whorled collagenPolarizationBirefringent particles
AsbestosisLower fibrosis/plaques → asbestos bodiesPrussian blueFerruginous body + fibrosis
SarcoidBilateral hilar nodes → noncaseating granulomasNegative organism studiesNo caseation/organism
PE/infarctPeripheral hemorrhagic wedge → ischemic hemorrhagic necrosisCTPA/D-dimerWedge/vessel relation
GoodpastureDiffuse hemorrhagic lung → siderophagesLinear IgG IFLinear vs granular
CAP lobarRed→gray hepatization → alveolar neutrophils/fibrinGram/cultureAlveolar suppuration
HAPPatchy consolidation → neutrophilsCultureHospital context
AspirationDependent consolidation → foreign material/giant cellsH&E/cultureAspirated material
Lung abscessPus-filled cavity → suppurative necrosisCultureNo malignant cavity-wall cells
HistoplasmaGranulomatous nodule → intracellular yeastGMS/PASFungal stain vs AFB
AdenocarcinomaPeripheral mass → glands/mucinTTF-1/Napsin AGlandular phenotype
Squamous CACentral/cavitating mass → keratin pearlsp40/p63Squamous differentiation
Small cell CACentral bulky tumor → nuclear moldingNeuroendocrine markersSmall blue neuroendocrine morphology
Pleural effusionPleural fluid → cause-dependentThoracentesisFluid outside lung
PneumothoraxPleural air/collapsed lungImagingAir, not fluid
MesotheliomaPleural rind → epithelioid/sarcomatoid/biphasicCalretinin/WT1/CK5/6Claudin-4 negative

▪Final 20-Second Board Recall

  • Atelectasis: volume loss; resorption shifts mediastinum toward, compression away.
  • Pulmonary edema: heavy wet lungs + heart-failure cells.
  • ARDS: heavy red firm lungs + hyaline membranes + type II pneumocyte repair.
  • Emphysema: enlarged distal airspaces + destroyed alveolar septa.
  • Chronic bronchitis: mucous-gland hyperplasia → Reid index ↑.
  • Asthma: mucus plugs + Curschmann spirals + Charcot-Leyden crystals + eosinophils + smooth-muscle hypertrophy.
  • Bronchiectasis: permanent cystic bronchial dilation + mucopurulent contents.
  • UIP: subpleural/basal honeycombing + temporal heterogeneity + fibroblastic foci.
  • Silicosis: upper lobe + whorled collagen + birefringent particles.
  • Asbestosis: lower/subpleural fibrosis + ferruginous bodies; asbestos bodies alone only indicate exposure.
  • Sarcoid: noncaseating granulomas; asteroid/Schaumann bodies are not pathognomonic.
  • Pulmonary infarct: peripheral wedge-shaped hemorrhagic lesion.
  • Goodpasture: alveolar hemorrhage + linear IgG.
  • Lobar pneumonia: red hepatization → gray hepatization.
  • Aspiration: dependent lesion + foreign material/giant cells.
  • Lung abscess: suppurative cavity; in an older adult, always exclude an obstructing carcinoma.
  • Adenocarcinoma: glands/mucin + TTF-1/Napsin A.
  • Squamous CA: central/cavitating + keratin pearls + p40/p63 → PTHrP hypercalcemia.
  • Small cell: nuclear molding + salt-and-pepper chromatin + neuroendocrine markers → SIADH/ACTH syndromes.
  • Pneumothorax: pleural air → collapsed lung.
  • Mesothelioma: pleural rind encasing lung + calretinin/WT1/CK5/6 positivity + Claudin-4 negativity.
  • Malignancy rule: IHC establishes lineage; stromal invasion, vascular/lymphatic invasion, or metastasis establishes malignant behavior.
Plate VIII — Respiratory DiseasesPathology Rapid Review · PLE Board
🩸IX
Regional block · 6% = 6 items covering anemia of blood loss, hemolytic anemia, anemia of diminished…

Hematopoietic and Oncologic Disorders

6% of Pathology PLE

▪Anemia of Blood Loss

Acute Blood Loss Anemia

Rapid-review elementFinding
GrossActive hemorrhage/bleeding source; no specific marrow gross lesion initially
MicroscopyInitially normocytic, normochromic RBCs; later polychromasia from reticulocytosis
Confirming stain/markerReticulocyte count ↑ during marrow recovery
Mimic killerHemolysis → has biochemical RBC-destruction pattern rather than a bleeding source
LabHb/Hct may initially appear near normal, then ↓ after hemodilution; reticulocytes subsequently ↑
Pathognomonic?No pathognomonic smear finding
Board trap: immediately after acute whole-blood loss, the hematocrit may not yet reflect the true RBC deficit.

    Chronic Blood Loss / Iron-Deficiency Anemia

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    GrossNo specific organ morphology; marrow may become red/hyperplastic
    MicroscopyMicrocytic hypochromic RBCs + increased central pallor + pencil cells
    Confirming stain/markerPrussian blue: absent stainable marrow iron DIAGNOSTIC SUPPORT
    Mimic killerAnemia of chronic inflammation → marrow/macrophage iron is preserved or increased
    LabFerritin ↓, Fe ↓, TIBC ↑, transferrin saturation ↓
    Pathognomonic?Pencil cells are characteristic, not pathognomonic; absent storage iron is much stronger evidence
    • Robbins specifically describes microcytic hypochromic RBCs, pencil cells, absent marrow macrophage iron on Prussian blue, low ferritin/iron, and high iron-binding capacity.

    ▪Hemolytic Anemias

    Hereditary Spherocytosis

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    GrossSplenomegaly ± black pigment gallstones
    MicroscopySmall dense spherocytes lacking central pallor + polychromasia
    Confirming stain/markerDirect Coombs negative; specialized membrane testing SUPPLEMENTARY
    Mimic killerWarm AIHA → also has spherocytes but Direct Coombs positive
    LabReticulocytes ↑, indirect bilirubin ↑, LDH ↑, haptoglobin ↓
    Pathognomonic?Spherocytes are characteristic but explicitly not pathognomonic
    • Robbins notes that spherocytes are dark, lack central pallor, and are also seen in autoimmune hemolytic anemia.

    G6PD-Deficiency Hemolytic Anemia

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    GrossUsually no specific organ lesion; acute attack may produce dark hemoglobinuric urine
    MicroscopyHeinz bodies → bite cells after splenic removal of inclusions
    Confirming stain/markerCrystal violet/supravital stain demonstrates Heinz bodies; enzyme assay confirms deficiency
    Mimic killerMAHA → produces schistocytes, not Heinz bodies/bite cells
    LabRetic ↑, indirect bilirubin ↑, LDH ↑, haptoglobin ↓ during attack
    Pathognomonic?Heinz bodies are oxidative-injury markers, not absolutely specific for G6PD deficiency

    Sickle Cell Disease

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    GrossChildhood splenomegaly → repeated infarction → autosplenectomy; multiple tissue infarcts
    MicroscopySickled RBCs + target cells + polychromasia; Howell-Jolly bodies with functional asplenia
    Confirming markerHemoglobin analysis/electrophoresis SUPPLEMENTARY
    Mimic killerThalassemia → target cells may occur, but sickled cells/HbS pattern distinguishes
    LabChronic anemia, reticulocytes ↑, bilirubin ↑ ± haptoglobin ↓
    Pathognomonic?Sickled RBCs are highly characteristic, but hemoglobin analysis confirms the hemoglobinopathy

    Warm Autoimmune Hemolytic Anemia

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    Gross± splenomegaly
    MicroscopySpherocytes + polychromasia
    Confirming markerDirect antiglobulin/Coombs test positive CONFIRMATORY
    Mimic killerHereditary spherocytosis → Coombs negative
    LabRetic ↑, LDH ↑, indirect bilirubin ↑, haptoglobin ↓
    Pathognomonic?Spherocytes are not specific; immune coating detected by Coombs is decisive

    Cold Agglutinin Disease

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    GrossNo characteristic organ lesion; acral cyanosis may occur clinically
    MicroscopyRBC agglutination in peripheral smear
    Confirming markerDirect Coombs usually demonstrates complement coating; cold-agglutinin testing SUPPLEMENTARY
    Mimic killerRouleaux → RBCs form orderly coin stacks rather than irregular agglutinated clumps
    LabHemolysis profile ± complement-mediated DAT positivity
    Pathognomonic?Agglutination is suggestive, not diagnostic alone

    ▪Anemia of Diminished Erythropoiesis

    Megaloblastic Anemia — B12/Folate Deficiency

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    GrossHypercellular red marrow; pernicious anemia may show gastric mucosal atrophy
    MicroscopyMacro-ovalocytes + hypersegmented neutrophils; marrow shows nuclear-cytoplasmic asynchrony
    Confirming markerB12/folate levels; methylmalonic acid ↑ favors B12 deficiency SUPPLEMENTARY
    Mimic killerNonmegaloblastic macrocytosis → lacks hypersegmented neutrophils/megaloblastic marrow
    LabMCV ↑, retic ↓; LDH may be markedly ↑ from ineffective erythropoiesis
    Pathognomonic?Macro-ovalocytes + hypersegmentation are highly characteristic, not individually pathognomonic
    • Robbins describes macro-ovalocytes, neutrophils with ≥5 lobes, hypercellular marrow, and nuclear-to-cytoplasmic asynchrony.

    Anemia of Chronic Inflammation

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    GrossNo specific gross lesion
    MicroscopyInitially normocytic; may become mildly microcytic; marrow macrophage iron preserved/increased
    Confirming stain/markerPrussian blue shows retained storage iron
    Mimic killerIron deficiency → storage iron absent
    LabFe ↓, TIBC ↓, ferritin normal/↑, reticulocyte response inadequate
    Pathognomonic?No pathognomonic smear finding
    • Rubin emphasizes normal/increased macrophage storage iron, low serum iron, and decreased TIBC in anemia of chronic disease.

    Aplastic Anemia

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    GrossPale/yellow fatty marrow; splenomegaly typically absent
    MicroscopyMarkedly hypocellular marrow replaced by fat with sparse residual hematopoietic cells
    Confirming testBone-marrow biopsy
    Mimic killerAcute leukemia → marrow is packed by blasts rather than empty/fatty
    LabPancytopenia + reticulocytopenia
    Pathognomonic?Profound fatty hypocellularity is the defining marrow pattern, after exclusion of mimics

    ▪Polycythemia

    Polycythemia Vera

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    GrossSplenomegaly; late/spent phase may produce massive splenomegaly
    MicroscopyHypercellular marrow with panmyelosis—erythroid, granulocytic, and megakaryocytic proliferation
    Confirming markerJAK2 mutation + serum EPO ↓
    Mimic killerSecondary polycythemia → EPO ↑ and lacks true panmyelosis
    LabHb/Hct ↑ ± WBC/platelets ↑; uric acid may ↑
    BehaviorMYELOPROLIFERATIVE NEOPLASM — malignant clonal disorder

    Secondary Polycythemia

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    GrossUnderlying hypoxic lung/heart disease or EPO-producing tumor may be evident
    MicroscopySelective erythroid marrow hyperplasia rather than trilineage panmyelosis
    Confirming markerSerum EPO ↑
    Mimic killerPV → EPO ↓ + JAK2 + panmyelosis
    LabHb/Hct ↑; oxygen saturation may ↓ in hypoxia-driven disease
    Pathognomonic?No specific smear morphology

    Relative Polycythemia

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    GrossNo marrow proliferative lesion
    MicroscopyRBC morphology/marrow essentially normal
    Confirming markerClinical evidence of reduced plasma volume
    Mimic killerAbsolute polycythemia → true red-cell mass increase
    LabHb/Hct ↑ from hemoconcentration
    Pathognomonic?None

    ▪Bleeding Disorders / Hemorrhagic Diathesis

    Immune Thrombocytopenic Purpura — Itp

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    GrossPetechiae/purpura; spleen usually not enlarged
    MicroscopyMarrow megakaryocytes ↑; peripheral blood shows thrombocytopenia with large young platelets
    Confirming markerNo sufficiently reliable platelet-antibody test; diagnosis by clinicopathologic exclusion
    Mimic killerDIC → PT/PTT abnormal; ITP has isolated thrombocytopenia with normal PT/PTT
    LabPlatelets ↓; PT normal; PTT normal
    Pathognomonic?Increased megakaryocytes are supportive, not specific

    TTP / HUS

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    GrossMultifocal microvascular ischemic/hemorrhagic lesions; kidney especially prominent in HUS
    MicroscopySchistocytes + platelet-rich hyaline microthrombi
    Confirming markerADAMTS13 activity severely ↓ in TTP SUPPLEMENTARY
    Mimic killerDIC → TTP/HUS usually has normal PT/PTT and fibrinogen
    LabPlatelets ↓, LDH ↑, haptoglobin ↓, indirect bilirubin ↑; PT/PTT generally normal
    Pathognomonic?Schistocytes are not pathognomonic; they indicate microangiopathic/mechanical hemolysis

    Von Willebrand Disease

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    GrossPredominantly mucocutaneous bleeding; no characteristic tissue lesion
    MicroscopyRoutine smear/marrow generally normal
    Confirming markervWF activity / ristocetin-dependent testing ↓
    Mimic killerHemophilia → deep hematomas/hemarthroses rather than predominantly mucosal bleeding
    LabPlatelet count normal; PT normal; PTT may be prolonged
    Pathognomonic?No pathognomonic morphology

    Hemophilia a

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    GrossRecurrent hemarthroses and deep muscle hematomas
    MicroscopyRecurrent joint bleeding → hemosiderin deposition, synovial hyperplasia and fibrosis
    Confirming markerFactor VIII activity ↓
    Mimic killerHemophilia B → factor IX activity ↓
    LabPTT ↑; PT normal; platelets normal
    Pathognomonic?Hemarthrosis is highly characteristic of severe coagulation-factor deficiency, not exclusive to hemophilia A

    Hemophilia B

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    GrossHemarthroses/deep hematomas
    MicroscopyChronic hemosiderin-rich destructive arthropathy
    Confirming markerFactor IX activity ↓
    Mimic killerHemophilia A → factor VIII ↓
    LabPTT ↑; PT normal; platelet count normal
    Pathognomonic?Morphology cannot distinguish A from B
    • Rubin emphasizes delayed bleeding, joint/muscle hemorrhage, and factor VIII versus IX deficiency as the distinction between hemophilia A and B.

    ▪Disseminated Intravascular Coagulation

    DIC

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    GrossSimultaneous thrombosis + petechiae/ecchymoses/oozing, with multiorgan ischemic or hemorrhagic lesions
    MicroscopyFibrin-platelet microthrombi in arterioles/capillaries/venules ± schistocytes
    Confirming markerConsumptive coagulation profile
    Mimic killerTTP/HUS → PT/PTT usually normal; DIC prolongs coagulation times and consumes fibrinogen
    LabPlatelets ↓, fibrinogen ↓, PT ↑, PTT ↑, D-dimer/FDP ↑
    Pathognomonic?Microthrombi alone are not unique; microthrombi + consumptive labs is the diagnostic pattern
    • Rubin defines DIC by systemic thrombin generation, microvascular fibrin thrombi, platelet/coagulation-factor consumption, fibrinolysis, and hemorrhagic diathesis.

    ▪Lymphoid Neoplasms

    Acute Lymphoblastic Leukemia / Lymphoblastic Lymphoma

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    BehaviorMALIGNANT, aggressive
    GrossHypercellular leukemic marrow; T-ALL may produce an anterior mediastinal/thymic mass
    MicroscopySmall/medium lymphoblasts with scant agranular cytoplasm and fine chromatin
    Confirming markerTdT; B-lineage: CD19/CD10/CD22; T-lineage: CD3 SUPPLEMENTARY
    Mimic killerAML → MPO/Auer rods establish myeloid differentiation
    LabAnemia, thrombocytopenia, neutropenia ± circulating blasts; total WBC may be high, normal, or low

    CLL / SLL

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    BehaviorMALIGNANT, usually indolent
    GrossGeneralized lymphadenopathy ± splenomegaly
    MicroscopySmall mature lymphocytes + smudge cells in blood; nodal proliferation centers
    Confirming markerCD19/CD20 + CD5 + CD23
    Mimic killerMantle-cell lymphoma → usually cyclin D1 positive and CD23 negative
    LabPersistent mature B-cell lymphocytosis
    Pathognomonic?Robbins states proliferation centers are pathognomonic when present; smudge cells alone are not
    • Robbins describes diffuse small lymphocytes, smudge cells, the CD5/CD23 B-cell phenotype, and calls proliferation centers pathognomonic when present.

    Follicular Lymphoma

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    BehaviorMALIGNANT, indolent B-cell lymphoma
    GrossGeneralized painless lymphadenopathy
    MicroscopyClosely packed neoplastic follicles lacking normal reactive polarization
    Confirming markerBCL2-positive follicles, CD10/BCL6 SUPPLEMENTARY
    Mimic killerReactive follicular hyperplasia → germinal centers normally lack BCL2 expression
    Lab/tumor markerNo specific serum tumor marker; LDH nonspecific
    Pathognomonic?Follicular architecture alone is not diagnostic; immunophenotype/clonality required

    Diffuse Large B-Cell Lymphoma — DLBCL

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    BehaviorMALIGNANT, aggressive
    GrossRapidly enlarging nodal or extranodal fleshy mass
    MicroscopyDiffuse architectural effacement by large atypical lymphoid cells
    Confirming markerCD20 with subclassification panel
    Mimic killerBurkitt → Burkitt has more monomorphic intermediate-sized cells + extreme starry-sky turnover
    LabLDH may ↑ but is nonspecific
    Pathognomonic?Large B cells alone are not sufficient; clonality/immunophenotype required

    Burkitt Lymphoma

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    BehaviorMALIGNANT, highly aggressive
    GrossEndemic: jaw mass; sporadic: ileocecal/abdominal mass
    MicroscopyMonomorphic intermediate-sized cells with extreme mitotic/apoptotic activity → “starry-sky”
    Confirming markerMYC rearrangement; germinal-center markers SUPPLEMENTARY
    Mimic killerDLBCL → Burkitt cells are smaller, highly monomorphic, and uniformly proliferative
    LabLDH/uric acid may ↑ from rapid turnover
    Pathognomonic?Starry sky is characteristic but NOT pathognomonic
    • Robbins describes the classic starry-sky appearance and the jaw-versus-ileocecal distribution of endemic versus sporadic Burkitt lymphoma.

    Classical Hodgkin Lymphoma

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    BehaviorMALIGNANT
    GrossEnlarged firm nodes; nodular sclerosis commonly presents with mediastinal lymphadenopathy
    MicroscopyReed-Sternberg cells in a polymorphous inflammatory background; classic RS cell has prominent “owl-eye” nucleoli
    Confirming markerCD15+, CD30+, weak PAX5+, CD45−
    Mimic killerReactive EBV immunoblast → isolated RS-like cells without correct architecture/IHC do not establish Hodgkin lymphoma
    LabLDH/ESR may ↑ but are nonspecific
    Pathognomonic?RS-like morphology alone is NOT pathognomonic; architecture + immunophenotype required
    • Robbins describes classic RS-cell morphology and the CD15/CD30/PAX5 immunophenotype.

    Plasma Cell Myeloma

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    BehaviorMALIGNANT plasma-cell neoplasm
    GrossMultiple sharply demarcated “punched-out” lytic skeletal lesions, classically skull/axial skeleton
    MicroscopySheets of plasma cells with eccentric clock-face nuclei and perinuclear hof; peripheral blood may show rouleaux
    Confirming markerCD138 + monoclonal light-chain restriction; serum/urine monoclonal protein
    Mimic killerMGUS → lacks myeloma-defining organ/bone injury
    LabM-protein, Ca²⁺ ↑, renal dysfunction, anemia; Bence Jones light chains may occur
    Pathognomonic?Rouleaux is not specific; clonal plasma cells + myeloma-defining injury is decisive
    • Robbins identifies punched-out bone lesions, hypercalcemia, renal failure, and other characteristic features of plasma-cell myeloma.

    ▪Myeloid Neoplasms

    Acute Myeloid Leukemia — AML

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    BehaviorMALIGNANT, aggressive
    GrossHypercellular marrow replaced by blasts ± hepatosplenomegaly; myeloid sarcoma may form a mass
    MicroscopyMyeloblasts with fine azurophilic granules; Auer rods may be present
    Confirming markerMyeloperoxidase (MPO), CD33 ± other myeloid markers
    Mimic killerALL → TdT/lymphoid markers and absence of MPO/Auer rods
    LabAnemia + thrombocytopenia + neutropenia ± circulating blasts
    Pathognomonic?Auer rods establish myeloid differentiation but do not alone specify AML subtype
    • Robbins describes myeloblasts with peroxidase-positive azurophilic granules and distinctive needle-shaped Auer rods.

    Acute Promyelocytic Leukemia — APL

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    BehaviorMALIGNANT AML subtype; hematologic emergency
    GrossMarrow replacement ± widespread hemorrhagic manifestations from DIC
    MicroscopyAbnormal heavily granulated promyelocytes; often multiple Auer rods
    Confirming markerPML-RARA / t(15;17) CONFIRMATORY
    Mimic killerOther AML → APL combines promyelocyte morphology + t(15;17)/PML-RARA + strong DIC association
    LabDIC pattern: platelets ↓, fibrinogen ↓, D-dimer ↑ ± PT/PTT ↑
    Pathognomonic?PML-RARA is disease-defining
    • Robbins illustrates t(15;17) APL with coarse granules, bilobed nuclei and cells containing multiple needle-like Auer rods.

    Chronic Myeloid Leukemia — CML

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    BehaviorMALIGNANT myeloproliferative neoplasm
    GrossMassive splenomegaly ± hepatomegaly
    MicroscopyMarked leukocytosis with full granulocytic maturation spectrum + basophilia; hypercellular granulocyte-rich marrow
    Confirming markerBCR-ABL1 / Philadelphia chromosome t(9;22) CONFIRMATORY
    Mimic killerLeukemoid reaction → BCR-ABL1 negative and typically lacks persistent marked basophilia
    LabWBC markedly ↑; basophils ↑ ± platelets ↑
    Pathognomonic?BCR-ABL1 in the appropriate clinicopathologic setting defines CML
    • Rubin describes CML with neutrophils in all stages of maturation, frequent basophilia/eosinophilia, and confirmation of the Philadelphia chromosome/BCR-ABL fusion by cytogenetics, FISH, or molecular methods.

    ▪Benign vs Malignant — Hematolymphoid Rule

    FeatureReactive / benign processHematolymphoid neoplasm
    PopulationPolymorphousOften monomorphic/clonal
    ArchitecturePreserved/reactiveEffaced or replaced
    MaturationAppropriate spectrumArrested or aberrant
    Flow/IHCExpected patternAberrant/clonal phenotype
    Light chainsPolytypicRestricted
    CytogeneticsNo defining cloneMay show disease-defining abnormality
    “Invasion”Not applicable as solid-tumor criterionNormal hematopoietic cells are inherently mobile
    MetastasisNot the usual conceptDissemination is intrinsic to many leukemias/lymphomas
    • Important: do not apply the solid-tumor rule “basement-membrane invasion = malignancy” to leukemia/lymphoma.
    • Leukemias: generally classified by lineage, maturation/blast burden, genetic abnormalities and response—not TNM.
    • Lymphomas: stage = anatomic distribution; biologic aggressiveness/grade is a separate concept.
    • Myeloma: malignant behavior is established by clonal plasma cells plus myeloma-defining disease, not merely by finding an M-protein.

    ▪Pathognomonic vs Characteristic — Board Safety

    FindingStrengthCorrect interpretation
    Microcytosis/hypochromiaSUGGESTIVEIDA, thalassemia and other causes

    ▪Final 20-Second Board Recall

    • Acute blood loss: initially normocytic; Hb/Hct may fall only after plasma-volume restoration.
    • Iron deficiency: microcytic hypochromic + pencil cells + ferritin↓ + TIBC↑ + marrow iron absent.
    • HS: spherocytes + splenomegaly + Coombs negative.
    • Warm AIHA: spherocytes + Coombs positive.
    • G6PD: Heinz bodies → bite cells.
    • Sickle disease: sickle cells + Howell-Jolly bodies + autosplenectomy.
    • Megaloblastic: macro-ovalocytes + hypersegmented neutrophils + nuclear-cytoplasmic asynchrony.
    • Anemia of inflammation: Fe↓ + TIBC↓ + ferritin normal/↑ + marrow iron retained.
    • Aplastic anemia: pancytopenia + retic↓ + empty fatty marrow.
    • PV: panmyelosis + JAK2 + EPO↓.
    • ITP: isolated platelet↓ + megakaryocytes↑ + PT/PTT normal.
    • TTP/HUS: platelet↓ + schistocytes + PT/PTT normal.
    • DIC: platelet↓ + fibrinogen↓ + PT/PTT↑ + D-dimer↑ + fibrin microthrombi.
    • vWD: mucosal bleeding + abnormal vWF/ristocetin activity.
    • Hemophilia: hemarthrosis + PTT↑ + PT normal.
    • ALL: TdT-positive lymphoblasts.
    • CLL: smudge cells + CD5/CD23 + proliferation centers.
    • Burkitt: jaw/ileocecal mass + starry sky, but starry sky is not pathognomonic.
    • Hodgkin: RS “owl-eye” cells + CD15/CD30 + weak PAX5.
    • Myeloma: punched-out lesions + plasma cells + rouleaux + M-protein + CRAB.
    • AML: Auer rods + MPO.
    • APL: multiple Auer rods + PML-RARA + DIC.
    • CML: massive splenomegaly + full granulocytic maturation + basophilia + BCR-ABL1.
    • Neoplasia rule: in hematolymphoid disease, clonality + abnormal architecture/maturation/immunophenotype/genetics establish malignancy; basement-membrane invasion is not the governing criterion.
    Plate IX — Hematopoietic and Oncologic DisordersPathology Rapid Review · PLE Board
    🪨X
    Regional block · 9% = 9 items covering glomerular…

    Genito-Urinary Disorders

    9% of Pathology PLE

    ▪Glomerular Diseases

    Acute Postinfectious Glomerulonephritis

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    GrossKidneys may be normal to mildly enlarged/pale; no diagnostic gross lesion
    MicroscopyDiffuse endocapillary hypercellularity + neutrophils; EM shows subepithelial humps
    Confirming stain/markerIF: granular IgG + C3 along GBM/mesangium
    Mimic killerIgA nephropathy → dominant mesangial IgA, not classic IgG/C3 humps
    LabHematuria, RBC casts, creatinine ↑, usually C3 ↓
    Pathognomonic?Humps are highly characteristic, not absolutely pathognomonic
    • Robbins and Rubin describe diffuse proliferative/neutrophilic glomerulonephritis with granular IgG/C3 and characteristic subepithelial “humps.”
    • Board timing: nephritic syndrome after a latent interval following infection favors postinfectious GN over synpharyngitic IgA nephropathy.

    Rapidly Progressive / Crescentic Glomerulonephritis

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    GrossOften enlarged/pale kidneys in active disease SUPPLEMENTARY
    MicroscopyCrescents compressing glomerular tufts ± necrosis/fibrin
    Confirming stain/markerType I = linear IgG/C3; Type II = granular IF; Type III = pauci-immune
    Mimic killerCrescents alone do not identify etiology—IF pattern does
    LabRapid creatinine ↑, hematuria, RBC casts; anti-GBM or ANCA according to subtype
    Pathognomonic?Crescents are NOT pathognomonic; they indicate severe glomerular injury
    • Robbins defines RPGN as a syndrome rather than a single disease and separates anti-GBM, immune-complex, and ANCA/pauci-immune patterns.

    Three-Way RPGN Discriminator

    TypeIFKey laboratory clue
    Type I — anti-GBMLinear IgG/C3Anti-GBM antibody
    Type II — immune complexGranularDisease-specific immune studies
    Type III — pauci-immuneLittle/no depositionPR3-ANCA or MPO-ANCA

    Iga Nephropathy — Berger Disease

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    GrossUsually grossly normal early
    MicroscopyMesangial widening/hypercellularity; EM mesangial deposits
    Confirming stain/markerDominant mesangial IgA ± C3 on IF CONFIRMATORY
    Mimic killerIgA vasculitis → renal biopsy may be identical; systemic purpura/arthralgia/GI symptoms distinguish
    LabRecurrent hematuria ± mild proteinuria; serum IgA may ↑ but is nonspecific
    Pathognomonic?Dominant renal mesangial IgA deposition is diagnostic in context
    • Robbins summarizes IgA nephropathy as mesangial proliferative disease with IgA ± C3 and mesangial/paramesangial dense deposits.

    Minimal Change Disease

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    GrossNormal-appearing kidneys
    MicroscopyLM essentially normal; EM shows diffuse podocyte foot-process effacement
    Confirming stain/markerIF negative
    Mimic killerFSGS → has focal segmental sclerosis/hyalinosis on adequately sampled LM
    LabNephrotic-range proteinuria, hypoalbuminemia; often relatively selective albuminuria
    Pathognomonic?Foot-process effacement is NOT pathognomonic; it occurs in other podocytopathies
    • Robbins' glomerular-disease summary lists normal LM, negative IF, and foot-process effacement without deposits in minimal-change disease.

    Focal Segmental Glomerulosclerosis

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    GrossUsually no specific early gross lesion
    MicroscopyFocal, segmental glomerular sclerosis + hyalinosis; EM foot-process effacement
    Confirming stain/markerPAS highlights sclerosis; IF may show nonspecific trapped IgM/C3
    Mimic killerMCD → no segmental scar; sampling error can miss FSGS
    LabHeavy proteinuria ± hematuria, hypertension and reduced GFR
    Pathognomonic?Focal segmental sclerosis is the defining histologic pattern, not a single etiology
    • Robbins summarizes FSGS as focal and segmental sclerosis/hyalinosis, often with focal IgM/C3 and foot-process effacement.

    Membranous Nephropathy

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    GrossUsually no characteristic gross lesion
    MicroscopyDiffuse capillary-wall/GBM thickening; EM subepithelial deposits
    Confirming stain/markerSilver stain: GBM spikes; IF: granular IgG/C3
    Mimic killerDiabetic nephropathy → mesangial nodules rather than discrete subepithelial immune deposits
    LabNephrotic-range proteinuria; anti-PLA2R may support primary disease SUPPLEMENTARY
    Pathognomonic?Spikes + subepithelial deposits are highly characteristic but require clinicopathologic correlation
    • Robbins identifies diffuse capillary-wall thickening, granular IgG/C3, and subepithelial deposits as the membranous pattern.

    Diabetic Nephropathy

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    GrossKidneys may be enlarged early; late chronic disease becomes scarred
    MicroscopyDiffuse mesangial sclerosis + Kimmelstiel-Wilson nodules + arteriolar hyalinosis
    Confirming stain/markerPAS strongly highlights mesangial matrix/nodules
    Mimic killerMembranous nephropathy → subepithelial deposits/spikes rather than PAS-positive mesangial nodules
    LabMicroalbuminuria first → overt proteinuria → declining GFR/creatinine ↑
    Pathognomonic?Kimmelstiel-Wilson nodules are highly characteristic, not absolutely unique

    ▪Tubular and Interstitial Diseases

    Acute Tubular Injury

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    GrossKidneys may be mildly swollen/pale SUPPLEMENTARY
    MicroscopyTubular epithelial flattening, necrosis/detachment, loss of brush border and casts
    Confirming stain/markerH&E; urine may show muddy-brown granular casts SUPPLEMENTARY
    Mimic killerAIN → interstitial lymphocytes/eosinophils rather than dominant epithelial necrosis/sloughing
    LabCreatinine ↑ ± oliguria; tubular concentrating defects
    Pathognomonic?Muddy-brown casts are strongly suggestive, not pathognomonic

    Acute Pyelonephritis

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    GrossPatchy yellow-white suppurative foci / wedge-shaped abscesses
    MicroscopyNeutrophils in tubules and interstitium — neutrophilic tubulitis
    Confirming stain/markerUrine culture; Gram stain as appropriate
    Mimic killerDrug AIN → eosinophil/lymphocyte-rich interstitial infiltrate rather than intratubular neutrophils
    LabPyuria, WBC casts, bacteriuria/culture positivity
    Pathognomonic?Neutrophilic tubulitis is highly characteristic of acute bacterial pyelonephritis

    Chronic Pyelonephritis / Reflux Nephropathy

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    GrossCoarse asymmetric/polar cortical scars overlying blunted/deformed calyces
    MicroscopyTubular atrophy/dilation with eosinophilic casts → “thyroidization” + interstitial fibrosis
    Confirming stain/markerH&E/PAS; no disease-specific IHC
    Mimic killerHydronephrosis → more uniform pelvicalyceal dilation/parenchymal thinning rather than focal polar scars
    LabConcentrating defect, recurrent pyuria/bacteriuria; creatinine ↑ late
    Pathognomonic?Thyroidization is characteristic but not specific
    • Robbins links chronic pyelonephritis with reflux/outflow obstruction and characteristic irregular scarring, particularly at the upper and lower poles.

    Drug-Induced Acute Interstitial Nephritis

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    GrossMild renal swelling may occur
    MicroscopyInterstitial edema + lymphocytes/macrophages ± eosinophils + tubulitis
    Confirming stain/markerNo specific stain; drug exposure + biopsy pattern
    Mimic killerAcute pyelonephritis → neutrophils within tubular lumina dominate
    LabCreatinine ↑, pyuria ± eosinophilia/eosinophiluria
    TimingOften develops days–weeks after offending drug
    Pathognomonic?Eosinophils are suggestive, not pathognomonic
    • Robbins describes drug AIN with prominent eosinophilic and mononuclear infiltrates and notes onset typically within 2–40 days after exposure.

    ▪Renal Vascular Disease

    Benign Hypertensive Nephrosclerosis

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    GrossBilaterally shrunken kidneys with fine “grain-leather” granular surface
    MicroscopyHyaline arteriolosclerosis + ischemic glomerulosclerosis/tubular atrophy
    Confirming stain/markerPAS highlights vascular hyaline/basement membranes
    Mimic killerChronic pyelonephritis → coarse irregular scars + distorted calyces
    LabMild proteinuria; slowly progressive creatinine ↑
    Pathognomonic?Hyaline arteriolosclerosis is characteristic but also occurs in diabetes/aging

    Malignant Hypertensive Nephrosclerosis

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    GrossCortical petechiae → classic “flea-bitten kidney” SUPPLEMENTARY
    MicroscopyOnion-skin hyperplastic arteriolosclerosis ± fibrinoid necrosis
    Confirming stain/markerH&E/PAS
    Mimic killerBenign hypertension → simple hyaline arteriolosclerosis rather than onion-skinning/necrosis
    LabRapid creatinine ↑ ± microangiopathic hemolysis
    Pathognomonic?Onion-skinning is highly characteristic of severe vascular injury, not exclusive to one disease

    ▪Cystic Renal Disease

    Autosomal Dominant Polycystic Kidney Disease

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    GrossMassively enlarged bilateral kidneys replaced by numerous variably sized cysts
    MicroscopyCysts from multiple nephron segments lined by flattened/cuboidal epithelium + intervening fibrosis
    Confirming marker/testImaging
    Mimic killerAcquired cystic disease → develops in longstanding ESRD, usually without classic massive inherited kidneys
    LabProgressive GFR ↓; hematuria may occur
    Pathognomonic?Gross distribution is highly characteristic, but imaging/family context confirms

    Autosomal Recessive Polycystic Kidney Disease

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    GrossLarge smooth kidneys with fine radial/spongelike cystic spaces
    MicroscopyDilated collecting ducts arranged perpendicular/radial to cortical surface
    Confirming marker/testImaging + associated hepatic disease
    Mimic killerADPKD → large irregular cysts of multiple nephron segments
    LabRenal dysfunction ± portal-hypertension findings from congenital hepatic fibrosis
    Pathognomonic?Radial collecting-duct dilation + congenital hepatic fibrosis is highly characteristic

    ▪Obstructive Uropathy

    Hydronephrosis

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    GrossDilated renal pelvis/calyces with progressive cortical/parenchymal thinning
    MicroscopyTubular dilation → tubular atrophy + interstitial fibrosis
    Confirming marker/testUltrasonography/imaging
    Mimic killerChronic pyelonephritis → coarse focal scars over distorted calyces, not uniform pressure dilation
    LabConcentrating defect early; azotemia/creatinine ↑ in bilateral or advanced obstruction
    Pathognomonic?Pelvicalyceal dilation is diagnostic of hydronephrosis but does not identify the obstructing cause

    ▪Renal Tumors

    Clear-Cell Renal Cell Carcinoma

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    BehaviorMALIGNANT
    GrossBright yellow-gray renal mass with hemorrhage/necrosis ± renal-vein extension
    MicroscopyNests/trabeculae of clear cells with delicate branching vasculature
    Confirming markerPAX8, CAIX, CD10 SUPPLEMENTARY support renal clear-cell phenotype
    Mimic killerXanthogranulomatous pyelonephritis → foamy macrophages/inflammation, not malignant clear epithelial nests
    Tumor marker/labNo reliable serum diagnostic marker; EPO may ↑ → polycythemia
    Malignant criterionDestructive invasion, renal-vein involvement, nodal or distant metastasis
    • RCC is a malignant epithelial tumor; tumor IHC establishes lineage but does not replace demonstration of malignant morphology/behavior.

    Wilms Tumor — Nephroblastoma

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    BehaviorMALIGNANT embryonal tumor
    GrossLarge, solitary, well-circumscribed soft tan-gray childhood renal mass ± hemorrhage/necrosis
    MicroscopyClassic triphasic pattern: blastemal + epithelial + stromal components
    Confirming markerWT1 SUPPLEMENTARY may support selected cases; morphology remains central
    Mimic killerNeuroblastoma → lacks nephrogenic triphasic blastema/tubules/stroma
    Tumor marker/labNo routine serum tumor marker
    Adverse featureAnaplasia: large hyperchromatic pleomorphic nuclei + abnormal mitoses
    • Robbins describes Wilms tumor as a well-circumscribed tan-gray renal mass and emphasizes the classic blastemal, epithelial, and stromal pattern; anaplasia predicts poorer treatment response.

    ▪Male Genital Disorders

    Benign Prostatic Hyperplasia

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    BehaviorBENIGN hyperplasia; not a premalignant lesion
    GrossTransition/periurethral nodules compress urethra into a slit-like lumen
    MicroscopyHyperplastic variably sized glands with papillary infolding + bland stroma; glands retain two cell layers
    Confirming markerBasal-cell markers demonstrate preserved basal layer SUPPLEMENTARY
    Mimic killerProstate carcinoma → basal-cell layer absent
    LabPSA may ↑ but is not cancer-specific
    Malignancy?No invasion or metastasis; BPH does not “turn into” adenocarcinoma
    • Robbins describes BPH transition-zone nodules, slit-like urethral compression and glands lined by secretory and basal-cell layers.

    Prostatic Adenocarcinoma

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    BehaviorMALIGNANT
    GrossUsually peripheral/posterior firm, gritty/indurated lesion
    MicroscopySmall crowded infiltrative glands lined by one cell layer, prominent nucleoli, absence of basal cells ± perineural invasion
    Confirming markerAMACR supports carcinoma; basal-cell markers are absent around malignant glands
    Mimic killerBPH/benign glands → retain basal-cell layer
    Tumor marker/labPSA useful clinically/for monitoring but has limited specificity
    Grade/stageGleason/Grade Group = architecture; stage = anatomic spread
    SpreadPelvic nodes; classic osteoblastic bone metastases
    • Robbins highlights absent basal cells, perineural invasion, AMACR as an ancillary marker, and osteoblastic skeletal metastases; it also cautions that ancillary stains can yield false-positive and false-negative results.

    ▪Testicular Germ-Cell Tumors

    Seminoma

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    BehaviorMALIGNANT germ-cell tumor
    GrossHomogeneous gray-white/fleshy lobulated mass, usually little hemorrhage or necrosis
    MicroscopySheets/lobules of uniform large clear cells separated by fibrous septa with lymphocytes
    Confirming markerOCT3/4, KIT, podoplanin; cytokeratin usually negative in classic seminoma
    Mimic killerEmbryonal carcinoma → more pleomorphic, poorly circumscribed and hemorrhagic/necrotic, usually cytokeratin+
    Tumor marker/labAFP should remain normal in pure seminoma; hCG may rise if syncytiotrophoblasts are present
    Pathognomonic?Clear-cell morphology is characteristic but requires IHC/clinical context

    Embryonal Carcinoma

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    BehaviorMALIGNANT, aggressive
    GrossVariegated, poorly circumscribed hemorrhagic/necrotic mass
    MicroscopyPleomorphic primitive cells in sheets/poorly formed glands with numerous mitoses
    Confirming markerOCT3/4+, cytokeratin+
    Mimic killerSeminoma → homogeneous tumor with uniform cells and lymphocyte-containing septa
    LabAFP/hCG depend on associated mixed germ-cell components
    Malignant criterionFrequent lymphovascular invasion
    • Robbins specifically contrasts embryonal carcinoma's hemorrhagic morphology with seminoma and notes pleomorphism, mitoses, and frequent vascular-lymphatic invasion.

    Yolk-Sac Tumor

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    BehaviorMALIGNANT germ-cell tumor
    GrossUsually solid testicular mass; relatively homogeneous in young children
    MicroscopyReticular/lace-like architecture + Schiller-Duval bodies + eosinophilic hyaline globules
    Confirming markerAFP in tumor cells/serum
    Mimic killerSeminoma → lacks Schiller-Duval bodies and does not raise AFP when pure
    LabAFP ↑
    Pathognomonic?Schiller-Duval bodies are highly characteristic, but occur in only a subset
    • Robbins describes Schiller-Duval bodies and AFP-containing hyaline globules as classic yolk-sac tumor findings.

    Choriocarcinoma

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    BehaviorHIGHLY MALIGNANT
    GrossOften relatively small primary tumor with extensive hemorrhage and necrosis
    MicroscopyMixed cytotrophoblasts + multinucleated syncytiotrophoblasts
    Confirming markerhCG strongly expressed by syncytiotrophoblasts
    Mimic killerOther germ-cell tumors lack the characteristic biphasic trophoblastic population
    LabhCG markedly ↑
    SpreadEarly hematogenous dissemination
    • Robbins emphasizes the biphasic trophoblastic morphology, intense hemorrhage/necrosis, and hCG-containing syncytiotrophoblasts.

    ▪Female Genital Disorders

    Endometriosis

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    BehaviorBENIGN, non-neoplastic, although locally infiltrative/fibrosing
    GrossRed-blue/brown implants; ovarian “chocolate cyst” filled with old blood
    MicroscopyEndometrial glands + endometrial stroma ± hemosiderin-laden macrophages outside uterine cavity
    Confirming markerH&E; CD10 SUPPLEMENTARY may highlight endometrial stroma
    Mimic killerEndosalpingiosis → lacks characteristic endometrial stroma/hemorrhage/hemosiderin
    LabNo diagnostic serum marker; CA-125 may rise nonspecifically
    Pathognomonic?Chocolate cyst is characteristic; histologic glands + stroma establish diagnosis
    • Robbins describes red-blue to yellow-brown lesions, adhesions, and ovarian “chocolate cysts” from recurrent hemorrhage.

    Cervical LSIL

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    BehaviorPREINVASIVE, not carcinoma
    GrossUsually no diagnostic gross lesion
    MicroscopyKoilocytosis with atypia largely in lower epithelial layers and preserved superficial maturation
    Confirming markerHPV testing; p16 may aid classification
    Mimic killerHSIL → atypical immature cells extend farther upward with greater loss of maturation
    LabHPV DNA may be positive
    Pathognomonic?Koilocytosis is suggestive of HPV effect, not invasive cancer

    Cervical HSIL / CIN II–III

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    BehaviorPREINVASIVE precursor lesion
    GrossUsually detected by screening/colposcopy rather than gross mass
    MicroscopyAtypical immature basal-type cells extend through increasing epithelial thickness; CIN III approaches full thickness
    Confirming markerBlock-like p16 + abnormal upward Ki-67 support HSIL
    Mimic killerInvasive SCC → breaches basement membrane and invades cervical stroma
    LabHigh-risk HPV test often positive
    Pathognomonic?p16 supports high-risk HPV biology but does not prove invasion

    Invasive Cervical Squamous Cell Carcinoma

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    BehaviorMALIGNANT
    GrossFungating/exophytic or infiltrative cervical mass ± ulceration
    MicroscopyNests/tongues of malignant squamous cells invading stroma ± keratinization
    Confirming markerp16 may support HPV-associated origin
    Mimic killerHSIL → atypical epithelium remains noninvasive
    Tumor marker/labNo serum marker replaces biopsy
    Definitive malignant criterionStromal invasion

    Endometrial Hyperplasia / Ein

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    BehaviorHyperplasia; atypical/EIN lesion is premalignant
    GrossDiffuse endometrial thickening
    MicroscopyIncreased gland-to-stroma ratio; atypical/EIN lesions show marked gland crowding/cytologic atypia
    Confirming markerTissue morphology; no single serum marker
    Mimic killerEndometrioid carcinoma → confluent malignant glands with desmoplasia/myometrial invasion
    LabN/A
    Pathognomonic?Gland crowding alone does not establish invasive carcinoma

    Endometrioid Endometrial Carcinoma

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    BehaviorMALIGNANT
    GrossPolypoid/fungating or diffuse endometrial mass
    MicroscopyCrowded malignant endometrioid glands ± solid growth with myometrial invasion
    Confirming markerMMR IHC SUPPLEMENTARY may classify/screen for mismatch-repair deficiency
    Mimic killerHyperplasia → retains more intervening stroma and lacks destructive invasion
    Tumor marker/labNo specific serum tumor marker
    Grade/stageGrade = glandular vs solid architecture; stage = anatomic extent

    Uterine Leiomyoma

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    BehaviorBENIGN
    GrossSharply circumscribed, firm gray-white “whorled” mass
    MicroscopyIntersecting fascicles of uniform spindle smooth-muscle cells, minimal atypia/mitoses
    Confirming markerSMA, desmin, h-caldesmon SUPPLEMENTARY establish smooth-muscle differentiation
    Mimic killerLeiomyosarcoma → significant atypia + mitoses + tumor-cell necrosis
    LabNo serum tumor marker
    Malignant potentialLeiomyomas generally do not transform into leiomyosarcoma
    • Robbins describes leiomyomas as sharply circumscribed, firm gray-white tumors composed of regular spindle smooth-muscle cells.

    Uterine Leiomyosarcoma

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    BehaviorMALIGNANT
    GrossBulky soft/fleshy hemorrhagic and necrotic uterine mass
    MicroscopySmooth-muscle tumor with cytologic atypia + increased mitoses + coagulative tumor-cell necrosis
    Confirming markerSMA/desmin/h-caldesmon support lineage but do not establish malignancy
    Mimic killerLeiomyoma → bland cells, few mitoses, no tumor-cell necrosis
    Tumor marker/labNo reliable serum marker
    SpreadPredominantly hematogenous SUPPLEMENTARY

    ▪Ovarian Serous Tumors

    Serous Cystadenoma

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    BehaviorBENIGN
    GrossThin-walled smooth glistening cyst ± simple papillary excrescences
    MicroscopySimple ciliated tubal-type epithelium without significant atypia/invasion
    Confirming markerN/A
    Mimic killerBorderline tumor → increased epithelial/papillary complexity
    LabCA-125 not diagnostically useful
    Malignant criterion absentNo stromal invasion

    Serous Borderline Tumor

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    BehaviorBORDERLINE / atypical proliferative tumor
    GrossCystic mass with numerous papillary projections
    MicroscopyComplex branching papillae + epithelial stratification/atypia without destructive stromal invasion
    Confirming markerMorphology; no serum marker confirms borderline behavior
    Mimic killerSerous carcinoma → destructive stromal invasion
    LabCA-125 may rise but is nonspecific
    Defining criterionNo destructive stromal invasion

    High-Grade Serous Carcinoma

    Rapid-review elementFinding
    BehaviorMALIGNANT
    GrossOften bilateral, complex solid-papillary/cystic masses with surface/peritoneal disease
    MicroscopyComplex papillary/solid growth, marked pleomorphism, atypical mitoses and destructive stromal invasion
    Confirming markerWT1, PAX8, aberrant p53 SUPPLEMENTARY
    Mimic killerBorderline tumor → lacks destructive stromal invasion
    Tumor marker/labCA-125 may aid monitoring but lacks sufficient specificity for primary diagnosis
    SpreadTranscoelomic/peritoneal dissemination → omental/peritoneal implants and ascites
    • Psammoma bodies are suggestive only; they do not establish malignancy.
    • Stromal invasion, not papillary architecture or psammoma bodies, establishes carcinoma.

    ▪Benign vs Malignant — GU Board Rules

    EntityBenign / preinvasiveMalignant discriminator
    BPH vs prostate carcinomaTwo-layer glands with basal cellsBasal-cell loss + infiltrative glands/perineural invasion
    Cervical HSIL vs SCCFull-thickness atypia may be severe but remains epithelialStromal invasion
    Leiomyoma vs leiomyosarcomaBland smooth muscle, low mitoses, no tumor necrosisAtypia + mitoses + tumor-cell necrosis
    Serous borderline vs carcinomaComplex papillae without destructive invasionDestructive stromal invasion
    Endometrial hyperplasia vs carcinomaCrowded glands without destructive invasionMyometrial/desmoplastic invasion
    Renal tumorsBenign morphology lacks destructive invasion/metastasisLocal invasion, venous invasion or metastasis
    Tumor IHCEstablishes cell lineageDoes not by itself prove malignant behavior
    Tumor markerMay be normal or elevated in benign diseaseDoes not replace histologic diagnosis

    Grade vs Stage

    Board trap: a cytologically ugly tumor does not automatically have a high stage; metastasis does.
    • Prostate: Gleason/Grade Group = microscopic architecture; stage = local/nodal/distant spread.
    • Endometrial carcinoma: grade = amount of gland formation versus solid growth; stage = extent of invasion/spread.
    • Wilms/RCC/ovarian/cervical tumors: stage reflects anatomic extent and is generally a major prognostic variable.

    ▪Pathognomonic vs Characteristic — Board Safety

    FindingStrengthCorrect interpretation
    Subepithelial humpsHIGHLY CHARACTERISTICPostinfectious GN; not absolutely unique
    Granular “lumpy-bumpy” IFPATTERN ONLYImmune-complex disease, not one diagnosis
    Linear IgGHIGHLY DIAGNOSTIC PATTERNAnti-GBM disease
    CrescentsNOT PATHOGNOMONICSevere glomerular injury
    Mesangial IgA dominanceDIAGNOSTIC IN CONTEXTIgA nephropathy / IgA vasculitis renal lesion
    Foot-process effacementNOT PATHOGNOMONICMCD, FSGS and other podocytopathies
    Focal segmental sclerosisDEFINING PATTERNFSGS pattern
    GBM spikes/subepithelial depositsHIGHLY CHARACTERISTICMembranous nephropathy
    Kimmelstiel-Wilson nodulesHIGHLY CHARACTERISTICDiabetic nephropathy
    Muddy-brown castsSUGGESTIVEAcute tubular injury
    Neutrophilic tubulitisHIGHLY CHARACTERISTICAcute pyelonephritis
    ThyroidizationCHARACTERISTIC, NOT SPECIFICChronic tubulointerstitial scarring
    Onion-skin arteriolesHIGHLY CHARACTERISTICSevere/malignant hypertension
    Yellow RCC massCHARACTERISTICClear-cell RCC; yellow lesions have mimics
    Triphasic Wilms patternHIGHLY CHARACTERISTICWilms tumor
    Basal-cell loss in prostate glandsSTRONG DIAGNOSTIC CLUEProstate carcinoma in correct architecture
    Perineural invasionHIGHLY SPECIFIC MALIGNANT CLUEProstatic carcinoma
    Schiller-Duval bodyHIGHLY CHARACTERISTICYolk-sac tumor
    Chocolate cystCHARACTERISTICEndometriosis
    KoilocytosisSUGGESTIVE OF HPV EFFECTNot invasive carcinoma
    Whorled uterine massHIGHLY CHARACTERISTICLeiomyoma
    Psammoma bodiesNOT PATHOGNOMONICCan occur in benign and malignant serous lesions
