    ▪Source Figure / Visual Audit

    • The primary TOS - Pathology file contains the examination blueprint but no disease-specific CNS gross specimens or photomicrographs; its Nervous System row defines the scope and cognitive allocation only.
    • Robbins Chapter 28 contains the relevant neuropathology gross photographs, histology, imaging and schematic figures; these are described below rather than reproduced verbatim.
    • Rubin similarly contains gross specimens, micrographs and imaging for cerebral edema/hydrocephalus, neurodegeneration, infection and CNS neoplasia; it is used here for corroborative visual description.

    ▪Visual / Photomicrograph Walk-Through

    What the Examinee Should Look for First

    Cerebral Infarction

    • Gross first: identify a vascular-territory region that is soft and eventually cavitary rather than a discrete solid mass.
    • Scanning power: look for abrupt transition from preserved cortex to pale necrotic tissue.
    • High power early: search for shrunken red neurons with intensely eosinophilic cytoplasm.
    • Subacute field: look for numerous foamy macrophages before searching for reactive astrocytes at the edge.
    • Remote field: identify tissue loss/cavity surrounded by gliosis.
    • Robbins' infarct figure specifically demonstrates acute eosinophilic neuronal injury, macrophage-rich subacute injury and an old cystic gliotic infarct.

    Lacunar Infarct

    • Gross first: search deep basal ganglia/internal capsule/pons for a small cavity rather than a large cortical wedge.
    • Scanning power: the cavity is bordered by gliotic tissue.
    • High power: inspect nearby arterioles for thick hyalinized walls.
    • First discriminator: deep <15-mm cavity in a hypertensive patient.
    • Robbins identifies these “lake-like” spaces as consequences of deep penetrating small-vessel disease.

    Hypertensive Hemorrhage vs CAA

    • Gross hypertensive: look centrally—putamen/thalamus/pons—for a large hematoma.
    • Gross CAA: look superficially for a lobar cortical/subcortical hemorrhage.
    • High power HTN: identify hyaline arteriolosclerosis.
    • High power CAA: inspect cortical/leptomeningeal vessels for amyloid.
    • Aβ IHC: highlights vascular deposits in CAA.

    Saccular Aneurysm

    • Gross first: expose the circle of Willis and search arterial branch points for a thin-walled berry-like sac.
    • Cut section: wall is markedly thinner than the parent artery.
    • High power: normal media/internal elastic structures are deficient.
    • After rupture: look for diffuse subarachnoid blood coating basal cisterns.
    • DΦFirst board clue: spontaneous basal SAH → immediately inspect circle-of-Willis branch points.

    Cerebral Edema / Herniation

    • Gross edema: broadened flattened gyri + narrowed sulci.
    • Subfalcine: cingulate gyrus displaced beneath falx.
    • Uncal: medial temporal lobe displaced through tentorial notch.
    • Tonsillar: cerebellar tonsils displaced through foramen magnum.
    • First thing to determine: which compartment boundary the brain has crossed; this predicts the compressed vascular/cranial-nerve structure.

    Hydrocephalus

    • Gross first: compare sizes of lateral, third and fourth ventricles.
    • Noncommunicating: identify the level where enlargement stops.
    • Communicating: generalized ventricular enlargement favors resorption failure.
    • Low power: periventricular white matter may show transependymal/interstitial edema.
    • First board distinction: dilated ventricle = hydrocephalus; compressed ventricle = diffuse cerebral edema.

    Pyogenic Meningitis

    • Gross: purulent material fills sulci and coats leptomeninges.
    • Scanning: inflammation lies primarily in the subarachnoid space, not within brain parenchyma.
    • High power: dense neutrophilic exudate surrounds leptomeningeal vessels.
    • Gram stain: search for organisms before antibiotics alter yield.
    • DΦFirst laboratory clue: CSF neutrophils↑ + glucose↓ + protein↑.

    Brain Abscess

    • Gross: central soft necrotic cavity surrounded by swollen brain.
    • DΦScanning: central necrosis → vascular granulation tissue → collagenous capsule → outer reactive gliosis.
    • High power: neutrophils dominate the active suppurative center.
    • Outside capsule: search for gemistocytic astrocytes.
    • First mimic check: viable malignant cells around necrosis suggest glioblastoma rather than abscess.

    HSV Encephalitis

    • Gross: inspect inferior/medial temporal and orbital frontal lobes for hemorrhagic destruction.
    • Scanning: gray and white matter contain irregular zones of hemorrhagic necrosis.
    • Low power: perivascular lymphocytic cuffs and microglial nodules.
    • High power: identify neuronophagia and Cowdry A intranuclear inclusions.
    • First board clue: hemorrhagic temporal encephalitis.

    Multiple Sclerosis

    • Gross: seek sharply demarcated plaques around ventricles, optic pathways, brainstem and spinal cord.
    • Scanning active plaque: hypercellularity reflects macrophage accumulation.
    • Luxol fast blue: plaque becomes sharply pale because myelin is absent.
    • Neurofilament: axons remain relatively preserved early.
    • First discriminator: myelin loss out of proportion to axonal loss.

    Alzheimer Disease

    • Gross: narrowed gyri + widened sulci + prominent medial temporal/hippocampal atrophy.
    • Scanning: cortex shows progressive neuronal loss and gliosis.
    • High power: locate neuritic plaques, then search neurons for tangles.
    • Congo red/Aβ: highlights plaque cores; phospho-tau highlights tangles.
    • First board rule: ventricular enlargement is ex vacuo, not obstructive hydrocephalus.

    Parkinson Disease

    • Gross: compare midbrain substantia nigra with normal heavily pigmented tissue; affected nigra is conspicuously pale.
    • Scanning: neuronal density in substantia nigra is reduced with gliosis.
    • High power: remaining neurons may contain eosinophilic Lewy bodies with pale halos.
    • α-Synuclein IHC: highlights Lewy bodies/neurites.
    • First clue: depigmented substantia nigra + neuronal Lewy bodies.

    Glioblastoma

    • Gross: locate an infiltrative red-yellow-gray variegated mass with hemorrhage and necrosis.
    • Scanning: look specifically for serpiginous necrotic zones.
    • High power: malignant tumor cells align around necrosis—pseudopalisading.
    • Next: search for endothelial/microvascular proliferation.
    • First board rule: necrosis + microvascular proliferation = high-grade astrocytic morphology in the cited Robbins framework.

    Oligodendroglioma

    • Gross: gray gelatinous cerebral mass; calcification may be evident.
    • Scanning: uniform tumor population with delicate vasculature.
    • High power: round nuclei + clear halos = fried egg.
    • Vessels: branching thin capillaries = chicken wire.
    • First confirmation: correlate morphology with IDH/1p19q diagnostic testing.

    Ependymoma

    • Gross: intraventricular mass, classically fourth ventricular in a child.
    • Scanning: relatively circumscribed tumor around abundant vessels.
    • DΦHigh power: cell processes radiate toward vessels → perivascular pseudorosettes.
    • EMA: characteristic dot/ring cytoplasmic pattern; GFAP often highlights perivascular processes.
    • First board clue: fourth-ventricular mass + pseudorosettes.

    Medulloblastoma

    • Gross: midline/vermis cerebellar tumor compressing fourth ventricle.
    • Scanning: dense sheet of primitive blue cells.
    • High power: scant cytoplasm, hyperchromatic nuclei, mitoses/apoptosis.
    • Rosette: Homer-Wright rosette has central neuropil rather than a vascular core/lumen.
    • DΦFirst complication to remember: CSF dissemination → drop metastases.

    Meningioma

    • Gross: rounded dura-based mass with broad attachment compressing brain.
    • Scanning: cohesive meningothelial sheets/fascicles.
    • High power: search for whorls, then laminated psammoma bodies.
    • EMA: supports meningothelial differentiation.
    • First board clue: extra-axial dural mass + whorls/psammoma bodies.

    ▪Key Morphologic Points

    • DPCerebral infarction: red neurons → neutrophils → macrophages → gliosis → cystic cavity; brain infarction undergoes liquefactive necrosis.
    • Lacunar infarct: <15-mm deep cavitary infarct from hypertensive penetrating-vessel disease.
    • Hypertension: deep putaminal/thalamic/pontine hemorrhage + hyaline arteriolosclerosis.
    • CAA: lobar hemorrhage + Aβ-containing cortical/leptomeningeal vessels.
    • DΦSaccular aneurysm: thin-walled circle-of-Willis branch-point aneurysm → spontaneous SAH.
    • Edema: flattened gyri + narrowed sulci; cytotoxic = cellular, vasogenic = BBB leakage, interstitial = hydrocephalic CSF flow.
    • Hydrocephalus: ventricles dilate; edema usually compresses them.
    • DΦHerniation: subfalcine → ACA; uncal → CN III/PCA/brainstem; tonsillar → medulla.
    • DΦPyogenic meningitis: purulent leptomeninges + subarachnoid neutrophils + CSF glucose↓.
    • DΦBrain abscess: central liquefactive necrosis → vascular granulation tissue → collagenous capsule → outer gliosis.
    • HSV: hemorrhagic temporal-lobe necrosis + lymphocytic cuffs + microglial nodules ± Cowdry A inclusions.
    • TB meningitis: basal granulomatous meningitis + low-glucose lymphocytic CSF.
    • MS: active macrophage-rich demyelinating plaque + Luxol-fast-blue myelin loss + relative axonal preservation.
    • Alzheimer: cortical/hippocampal atrophy + neuritic Aβ plaques + tau tangles + hydrocephalus ex vacuo.
    • Parkinson: pale substantia nigra + α-synuclein Lewy bodies.
    • CJD: spongiform vacuolation + rapidly progressive dementia.
    • Glioblastoma: hemorrhagic-necrotic infiltrative mass + pseudopalisading necrosis + microvascular proliferation.
    • Oligodendroglioma: fried-egg cells + chicken-wire vessels + calcification, integrated with IDH/1p19q status.
    • Ependymoma: perivascular pseudorosettes + dot/ring EMA, often fourth ventricular in children.
    • Medulloblastoma: cerebellar small-blue-cell tumor + Homer-Wright rosettes + CSF drop metastases.
    • Meningioma: dural-based compressive mass + whorls + psammoma bodies + EMA.

    ▪Key Clinical Points — Pathogenesis → Morphology → Sign → Lab

    • DPArterial occlusion → ATP depletion/excitotoxic injury → red neurons/liquefactive infarction → abrupt focal deficit → MRI vascular-territory lesion.
    • DPChronic hypertension → penetrating-vessel hyalinosis → deep lacune → pure motor/sensory syndrome → small deep infarct on imaging.
    • DΦHypertensive vessel rupture → deep hematoma → internal-capsule/basal-ganglia destruction → sudden hemiparesis ± ICP elevation.
    • DΦCAA → amyloid-weakened cortical vessels → lobar hemorrhage → recurrent focal neurologic deficits → Aβ-positive vessels on tissue.
    • DΦSaccular aneurysm rupture → arterial blood in subarachnoid space → meningeal irritation/ICP surge → thunderclap headache → CT/CSF evidence of SAH.
    • DΦBBB disruption → vasogenic edema → increased brain volume → headache/papilledema → imaging shows perilesional edema.
    • DPCSF outflow/resorption failure → ventricular dilation → ICP ↑ → headache/vomiting → imaging localizes obstructive versus communicating hydrocephalus.
    • DΦExpanding mass/edema → pressure gradient → uncal herniation → CN III compression → ipsilateral fixed dilated pupil.
    • DΦPyogenic infection → neutrophilic leptomeningitis → neck stiffness/altered consciousness → CSF neutrophils↑, protein↑, glucose↓.
    • DΦParenchymal suppuration → liquefactive abscess + vasogenic edema → headache/focal deficit → ring lesion with infectious morphology/culture.
    • DΦHSV replication → hemorrhagic temporal necrosis → behavioral change/seizures → CSF HSV PCR positivity.
    • DPTB granulomatous basilar meningitis → arterial/CSF-pathway compromise → cranial neuropathy/infarct/hydrocephalus → lymphocytic low-glucose CSF + microbiologic confirmation.
    • DΦImmune attack on myelin → plaques with early axonal preservation → relapsing multifocal deficits → MRI dissemination + CSF oligoclonal bands.
    • DΦAβ/tau accumulation → hippocampal/cortical neuronal loss → progressive memory/cognitive decline → CSF Aβ↓/phospho-tau↑ with characteristic neuropathology.
    • DΦNigral neuron loss → dopamine depletion → bradykinesia/rigidity/tremor → pale substantia nigra + α-synuclein Lewy bodies.
    • DΦPrion propagation → spongiform neuronal injury → rapidly progressive dementia/myoclonus → RT-QuIC SUPPLEMENTARY + spongiform pathology.
    • DΦGlioblastoma angiogenesis/necrosis → infiltrative hemorrhagic tumor + edema → seizures/focal deficits/ICP → tissue shows pseudopalisading necrosis and microvascular proliferation.
    • DΦOligodendroglioma cortical infiltration → slow-growing calcified hemispheric tumor → seizures → fried-egg/chicken-wire morphology + diagnostic molecular profile.
    • DPFourth-ventricular ependymoma → CSF obstruction → hydrocephalus → vomiting/headache → perivascular pseudorosettes with GFAP/EMA.
    • DPCerebellar medulloblastoma → rapid embryonal growth → fourth-ventricle obstruction/ataxia → small-blue-cell/Homer-Wright morphology + possible CSF drop metastases.
    • DΦDural meningothelial growth → extra-axial compression → seizures/focal dysfunction → whorls/psammoma bodies with EMA-positive tumor.
    • DΦFinal PLE synthesis: with 4 Applying + 1 Analyzing + 1 Evaluating item, prioritize anatomic location → dominant tissue reaction → time course → stain/CSF/imaging confirmation, then decide whether the finding is diagnostic, merely characteristic, or a common distractor.
    Plate XII — Nervous System DisordersPathology Rapid Review · PLE Board
    🏁✦
    Cross-cutting · board-recall lines and one-look grids from all twelve plates

    Final Sprint

    TOS priority closing sequence

    🏁Final Board Recall — All Systems

    🔬 Compressed Rapid-Review
    • Swollen cell → reversible injury/necrosis pathway; shrunken cell → apoptosis.
    • Ghost architecture → coagulative necrosis.
    • Brain + liquefaction → liquefactive necrosis.
    • Cheesy → caseous necrosis; chalky white → fat necrosis.
    • Pus + neutrophils → suppurative inflammation.
    • Epithelioid histiocytes → granuloma.
    • Capillaries + fibroblasts → granulation tissue.
    • Heart failure cells + Prussian blue → chronic pulmonary congestion.
    • Nutmeg liver → chronic passive hepatic congestion.
    • Lines of Zahn → antemortem thrombus.
    • Red-blue peripheral wedge in lung → hemorrhagic pulmonary infarct.
    • Type I → IgE/eosinophilic late phase; II → antibody to cell/tissue; III → immune complex/fibrinoid necrosis; IV → T cells/granulomas.
    • Lactate ↑ + multiorgan hypoperfusion → shock.
    • Hyaline membranes → diffuse alveolar damage, not uniquely septic shock.
    🧬 Developmental Disorders and Neoplasia
    • Marfan → arachnodactyly + ectopia lentis + cystic medial degeneration → characteristic, not pathognomonic.
    • Tay-Sachs → cherry-red spot + ballooned neurons + whorled lysosomes → confirm Hexosaminidase A deficiency.
    • Down = +21; Patau = +13; Edwards = +18.
    • 22q11.2 deletion → cardiac outflow defect + thymic hypoplasia + Ca²⁺ ↓.
    • Klinefelter → small testes + hyalinized seminiferous tubules + FSH/LH ↑ → 47,XXY.
    • Turner → streak ovaries + FSH/LH ↑ → 45,X spectrum.
    • Fragile X → macro-orchidism → confirm FMR1 repeat expansion.
    • Prader-Willi = paternal-expression loss; Angelman = maternal-expression loss.
    • Benign = circumscribed + differentiated + noninvasive + never metastatic.
    • Malignant = invasive ± anaplastic ± metastatic.
    • Metastasis = definitive malignant behavior.
    • CIS = atypia without stromal invasion; invasive carcinoma = basement-membrane breach with tumor in stroma.
    • Grade = microscope; Stage = spread.
    • Tumor markers support and monitor disease; they do not replace morphology/biopsy.
    🛡️ Immunopathology
    • Type I: edema/congestion immediately → eosinophil-rich infiltrate later; eosinophilia is characteristic, not pathognomonic.
    • Type II anti-GBM: linear IgG ± C3 along GBM; this is the single most important morphology-based discriminator.
    • Type III: granular immune deposits + fibrinoid necrotizing vasculitis.
    • Type IV: T-cell/macrophage inflammation ± granuloma, with delayed rather than immediate onset.
    • Linear IF = fixed basement-membrane antigen; granular IF = deposited immune complexes.
    • SLE: wire-loop glomerular capillaries, granular IF, butterfly rash, Libman-Sacks vegetations.
    • Wire loops are characteristic of severe lupus nephritis, but the diagnosis still requires clinicopathologic/serologic correlation.
    • Lupus-band-type dermoepidermal Ig deposition is not specific for SLE.
    • RA: pannus is the key joint lesion; pannus destroys cartilage and subchondral bone.
    • Rheumatoid nodule: central fibrinoid necrosis + palisading macrophages.
    • RA vs OA: inflammatory pannus/erosions versus cartilage fibrillation, osteophytes, and subchondral sclerosis.
    • Sjögren: dense periductal lymphocytes → acinar destruction → fibrosis/fat; epimyoepithelial islands are a classic microscopic phrase.
    • Sjögren vs lymphoma: polymorphous reactive lymphoid tissue versus destructive monoclonal B-cell expansion.
    • Systemic sclerosis: dense dermal collagen + adnexal atrophy + small-vessel disease.
    • Systemic sclerosis vs SLE: fibrosis/vascular narrowing dominates systemic sclerosis; immune-complex inflammation dominates many SLE lesions.
    • XLA: absent/underdeveloped germinal centers + absent plasma cells.
    • SCID: severely hypoplastic lymphoid tissue with profound T-cell ± B-cell depletion.
    • DiGeorge: small/absent thymus with T-cell deficiency and hypocalcemia.
    • Wiskott-Aldrich: progressive loss of lymph-node T-cell zones + thrombocytopenia/eczema.
    • Chédiak-Higashi: giant leukocyte cytoplasmic granules are the classic smear clue.
    • CGD: macrophage-rich granulomas are suggestive; the oxidative-burst assay, not H&E, establishes the functional defect.
    • Early HIV: follicular hyperplasia; late HIV: lymphoid depletion and “burnt-out” nodes.
    • AIDS infection trap: absence of granulomas does not exclude mycobacteria when CD4 cells are profoundly depleted.
    • Pathognomonic language: most immune morphologies are characteristic rather than pathognomonic because multiple immune diseases share common inflammatory patterns.
    • IgE/mast-cell activation → vascular leak + smooth-muscle contraction → urticaria/bronchospasm/hypotension; late eosinophilic inflammation sustains symptoms.
    • Anti-GBM antibody → linear basement-membrane attack → glomerular capillary rupture → hematuria/RBC casts; alveolar attack → hemoptysis.
    • Immune complexes → complement activation → neutrophilic vasculitis → fibrinoid necrosis; complement consumption → C3/C4 may fall.
    • T-cell hypersensitivity → delayed macrophage/T-cell inflammation → induration or granuloma rather than immediate wheal formation.
    • SLE immune complexes → wire-loop glomerular injury → proteinuria/hematuria/renal dysfunction; anti-dsDNA/Sm and complement changes support the diagnosis.
    • SLE valve immune injury → sterile Libman-Sacks vegetations; absence of destructive infectious colonies separates it from infective endocarditis.
    • RA synovitis → pannus → cartilage/bone erosion → deformity and ankylosis; anti-CCP/ACPA is the key serologic partner to the morphology.
    • Sjögren gland infiltration → acinar loss → tears/saliva ↓ → keratoconjunctivitis sicca + xerostomia; SSA/SSB support the diagnosis.
    • Persistent B-cell activation in Sjögren → increased lymphoma risk; a new dominant glandular mass should not be assumed to be benign autoimmune enlargement.
    • Systemic-sclerosis vascular injury → Raynaud; fibrosis → taut skin, esophageal dysmotility, pulmonary fibrosis, myocardial dysfunction; Scl-70/anticentromere help define phenotype.
    • SCID = broad pathogen vulnerability because both cellular and humoral defenses fail.
    • XLA = extracellular bacterial/enteroviral infection after 6 months because B cells and immunoglobulins are profoundly deficient while T-cell immunity remains relatively intact.
    • DiGeorge = T-cell deficiency + hypocalcemia + conotruncal cardiac lesion because thymus/parathyroids share developmental origins.
    • Wiskott-Aldrich = thrombocytopenia + eczema + recurrent infection; the laboratory pattern is part of the diagnosis because morphology is nonspecific.
    • CGD = neutrophils present but cannot kill normally → recurrent deep infection + granuloma; functional oxidative-burst testing is more useful than counting neutrophils.
    • HIV → early reactive follicular hyperplasia → progressive CD4 depletion → burnt-out lymphoid tissue → opportunistic infection and malignancy.
    • Low CD4 count changes morphology itself: severe infections may contain numerous organisms with a minimal inflammatory or granulomatous response.
    • Aging and malnutrition cause acquired immune decline without one diagnostic histologic lesion, so board questions should be solved through context, infection pattern, lymphoid atrophy, and laboratory/nutritional findings rather than a purported pathognomonic slide.
    • PLE priority: with 4 of 7 Immunopathology items at Applying level, always pair the finding: morphology → immune mechanism → clinical effect → confirmatory lab, e.g. wire loops → immune complexes → nephritis → anti-dsDNA/low complement.
    ☠️ Environmental and Nutritional Pathology
    • CO: cherry red + bilateral globus pallidus liquefactive necrosis → COHb ↑; pallidal injury is almost pathognomonic, not absolutely exclusive.
    • Lead: basophilic stippling + ring sideroblasts + wrist drop → blood lead ↑; Prussian blue highlights sideroblast iron.
    • Mercury: “mad as a hatter” + tremor/gingivitis + Purkinje-cell loss.
    • Arsenic: hyperpigmentation/hyperkeratosis of palms and soles → skin/lung/bladder cancer risk.
    • Cadmium: renal tubular toxicity + osteoporosis/osteomalacia = itai-itai.
    • Marasmus: wasting without edema.
    • Kwashiorkor: edema + hypoalbuminemia + fatty liver, with flaky-paint skin and flag sign as classic descriptors.
    • Vitamin A: Bitot spots / keratomalacia + keratinizing squamous metaplasia.
    • Rickets: wide disorganized growth plate.
    • Osteomalacia: thick unmineralized osteoid seams; unlike osteoporosis, mineralization itself is defective.
    • Scurvy: perifollicular hemorrhage + bleeding gums + poor wound healing.
    • Wernicke: mammillary-body petechial hemorrhage + confusion/ataxia/ocular findings.
    🦠 Infectious Diseases
    • HSV: vesicle + multinucleation + nuclear inclusions → PCR; H&E does not reliably separate HSV from VZV.
    • HBV: ground-glass hepatocyte → HBsAg/HBcAg.
    • EBV: atypical lymphocytes are activated host T cells; beware lymphoma mimic.
    • Pyogenic bacteria: pus = neutrophils + liquefactive necrosis.
    • TB: Ghon complex / caseating granuloma → AFB stain; caseation alone is not pathognomonic.
    • Syphilis: hard chancre + plasma cells + endarteritis → serology.
    • Clostridium: gas + blue-black friable muscle + scant neutrophils + boxcar gram-positive rods.
    • RMSF: palm/sole petechiae + endothelial vasculitis.
    • Candida: budding yeast + pseudohyphae.
    • Aspergillus: acute-angle septate.
    • Mucor: broad right-angle pauciseptate/nonseptate.
    • Malaria: Giemsa-positive intra-RBC parasite + hemozoin.
    • Amebiasis: flask-shaped ulcer + RBC-containing trophozoite.
    • Schistosomiasis: egg + eosinophilic granuloma → chronic fibrosis.
    • Tumor-marker rule: HBV/EBV infection can predispose to malignancy, but viral positivity or AFP elevation never substitutes for demonstration of malignant morphology, invasion, or metastasis.
    🍽️ Gastrointestinal Diseases
    • Omphalocele = covered; gastroschisis = uncovered.
    • Meckel = antimesenteric true diverticulum ± ectopic gastric mucosa.
    • Pyloric stenosis = olive + nonbilious vomiting + thick smooth muscle.
    • Hirschsprung = no ganglion cells.
    • Achalasia = bird beak + myenteric ganglion loss; dysphagia to solids and liquids.
    • GERD = basal hyperplasia + elongated papillae + eosinophils.
    • Varices = dilated distal submucosal veins → massive hematemesis.
    • Barrett = red velvety mucosa + goblet cells → adenocarcinoma risk.
    • PUD = punched-out clean crater; carcinoma = irregular invasive ulcer.
    • H. pylori = surface organisms + chronic-active gastritis + lymphoid follicles.
    • ZES = recurrent distal ulcers + gastrin ↑.
    • Diffuse gastric CA = linitis plastica + signet-ring cells.
    • Volvulus = twisting; intussusception = telescoping.
    • Celiac = villous atrophy + crypt hyperplasia + IELs.
    • Crohn = skip + cobblestone + transmural + fistula/stricture ± granuloma.
    • UC = continuous rectal disease + crypt abscesses + pseudopolyps; granulomas absent.
    • Adenoma = dysplastic but noninvasive; submucosal invasion = colorectal adenocarcinoma.
    • Appendicitis = neutrophils in muscularis propria.
    • Cirrhosis = regenerative nodules + bridging fibrosis.
    • PSC = onion-skin fibrosis.
    • Acute pancreatitis = chalky fat necrosis/saponification + lipase ↑.
    • Pancreatic adenocarcinoma = firm infiltrative mass + malignant glands + desmoplasia/perineural invasion.
    • Tumor-marker rule: CEA, AFP, and CA19-9 support surveillance/clinical correlation; none substitutes for demonstration of invasive malignant morphology.
    🫀 Cardiovascular and Collagen Disorders
    • Left HF: heavy wet lungs + heart-failure cells.
    • Right HF: nutmeg liver.
    • ASD/VSD/PDA: initially left → right; late irreversible pulmonary hypertension can produce Eisenmenger reversal.
    • TOF: VSD + RV outflow obstruction + overriding aorta + RVH → boot-shaped heart.
    • Coarctation: upper-body hypertension + weak femoral pulses + rib notching.
    • Angina: ischemia without necrosis; MI = necrosis + troponin rise.
    • MI: 1–3 d neutrophils; 3–7 d macrophages + rupture risk; weeks → collagen scar.
    • Hypertension: concentric LVH.
    • Cor pulmonale: acute = RV dilation; chronic = RV hypertrophy.
    • MVP: floppy/hooded valve + myxomatous spongiosa.
    • Acute rheumatic fever: Aschoff body + Anitschkow caterpillar cells + closure-line verrucae.
    • Chronic RHD: commissural fusion + shortened fused chordae → fish-mouth stenosis.
    • IE: large friable destructive vegetation + organisms.
    • Fibrinous pericarditis: bread-and-butter.
    • Constrictive pericarditis: concretio cordis.
    • DCM: flabby, four-chamber dilated heart + mural thrombi + nonspecific fibrosis.
    • HCM: asymmetric septal hypertrophy + banana LV + myofiber disarray.
    • Amyloid RCM: Congo red + apple-green birefringence.
    • Adult primary cardiac tumor: myxoma → gelatinous fossa-ovalis mass.
    • Childhood primary cardiac tumor: rhabdomyoma → spider cells.
    • Papillary fibroelastoma: sea-anemone valve lesion.
    • SLE: Libman-Sacks sterile vegetations; RA: palisading rheumatoid nodules; systemic sclerosis: patchy myocardial fibrosis; PAN: necrotizing coronary arteritis.
    • Tumor rule: embolization by a benign myxoma is not metastasis; destructive invasion or metastasis defines malignant cardiac tumor behavior.s
    🫁 Respiratory Diseases
    • Atelectasis: volume loss; resorption shifts mediastinum toward, compression away.
    • Pulmonary edema: heavy wet lungs + heart-failure cells.
    • ARDS: heavy red firm lungs + hyaline membranes + type II pneumocyte repair.
    • Emphysema: enlarged distal airspaces + destroyed alveolar septa.
    • Chronic bronchitis: mucous-gland hyperplasia → Reid index ↑.
    • Asthma: mucus plugs + Curschmann spirals + Charcot-Leyden crystals + eosinophils + smooth-muscle hypertrophy.
    • Bronchiectasis: permanent cystic bronchial dilation + mucopurulent contents.
    • UIP: subpleural/basal honeycombing + temporal heterogeneity + fibroblastic foci.
    • Silicosis: upper lobe + whorled collagen + birefringent particles.
    • Asbestosis: lower/subpleural fibrosis + ferruginous bodies; asbestos bodies alone only indicate exposure.
    • Sarcoid: noncaseating granulomas; asteroid/Schaumann bodies are not pathognomonic.
    • Pulmonary infarct: peripheral wedge-shaped hemorrhagic lesion.
    • Goodpasture: alveolar hemorrhage + linear IgG.
    • Lobar pneumonia: red hepatization → gray hepatization.
    • Aspiration: dependent lesion + foreign material/giant cells.
    • Lung abscess: suppurative cavity; in an older adult, always exclude an obstructing carcinoma.
    • Adenocarcinoma: glands/mucin + TTF-1/Napsin A.
    • Squamous CA: central/cavitating + keratin pearls + p40/p63 → PTHrP hypercalcemia.
    • Small cell: nuclear molding + salt-and-pepper chromatin + neuroendocrine markers → SIADH/ACTH syndromes.
    • Pneumothorax: pleural air → collapsed lung.
    • Mesothelioma: pleural rind encasing lung + calretinin/WT1/CK5/6 positivity + Claudin-4 negativity.
    • Malignancy rule: IHC establishes lineage; stromal invasion, vascular/lymphatic invasion, or metastasis establishes malignant behavior.
    🩸 Hematopoietic and Oncologic Disorders
    • Acute blood loss: initially normocytic; Hb/Hct may fall only after plasma-volume restoration.
    • Iron deficiency: microcytic hypochromic + pencil cells + ferritin↓ + TIBC↑ + marrow iron absent.
    • HS: spherocytes + splenomegaly + Coombs negative.
    • Warm AIHA: spherocytes + Coombs positive.
    • G6PD: Heinz bodies → bite cells.
    • Sickle disease: sickle cells + Howell-Jolly bodies + autosplenectomy.
    • Megaloblastic: macro-ovalocytes + hypersegmented neutrophils + nuclear-cytoplasmic asynchrony.
    • Anemia of inflammation: Fe↓ + TIBC↓ + ferritin normal/↑ + marrow iron retained.
    • Aplastic anemia: pancytopenia + retic↓ + empty fatty marrow.
    • PV: panmyelosis + JAK2 + EPO↓.
    • ITP: isolated platelet↓ + megakaryocytes↑ + PT/PTT normal.
    • TTP/HUS: platelet↓ + schistocytes + PT/PTT normal.
    • DIC: platelet↓ + fibrinogen↓ + PT/PTT↑ + D-dimer↑ + fibrin microthrombi.
    • vWD: mucosal bleeding + abnormal vWF/ristocetin activity.
    • Hemophilia: hemarthrosis + PTT↑ + PT normal.
    • ALL: TdT-positive lymphoblasts.
    • CLL: smudge cells + CD5/CD23 + proliferation centers.
    • Burkitt: jaw/ileocecal mass + starry sky, but starry sky is not pathognomonic.
    • Hodgkin: RS “owl-eye” cells + CD15/CD30 + weak PAX5.
    • Myeloma: punched-out lesions + plasma cells + rouleaux + M-protein + CRAB.
    • AML: Auer rods + MPO.
    • APL: multiple Auer rods + PML-RARA + DIC.
    • CML: massive splenomegaly + full granulocytic maturation + basophilia + BCR-ABL1.
    • Neoplasia rule: in hematolymphoid disease, clonality + abnormal architecture/maturation/immunophenotype/genetics establish malignancy; basement-membrane invasion is not the governing criterion.
    🪨 Genito-Urinary Disorders
    • Postinfectious GN: neutrophilic hypercellularity + granular IgG/C3 + subepithelial humps + low C3.
    • RPGN: crescent = severity; linear = anti-GBM, granular = immune complex, pauci-immune = ANCA.
    • IgA nephropathy: mesangial IgA.
    • MCD: normal LM + negative IF + foot-process effacement.
    • FSGS: focal segmental sclerosis/hyalinosis; beware sampling error.
    • Membranous: GBM spikes + subepithelial deposits + granular IgG/C3.
    • Diabetic nephropathy: PAS-positive Kimmelstiel-Wilson nodules; microalbuminuria appears first.
    • ATI: tubular epithelial injury + muddy-brown casts.
    • Acute pyelo: neutrophilic tubulitis + WBC casts.
    • Chronic pyelo: coarse polar scars + blunted calyces + thyroidization.
    • Drug AIN: drug exposure + eosinophils/lymphocytes + tubulitis.
    • Benign HTN: grain-leather + hyaline arteriolosclerosis.
    • Malignant HTN: flea-bitten + onion-skinning ± fibrinoid necrosis.
    • ADPKD: massive bilateral multicystic kidneys; ARPKD = radial collecting-duct dilations + congenital hepatic fibrosis.
    • Hydronephrosis: dilated pelvis/calyces + thinned cortex.
    • Clear-cell RCC: yellow renal mass + clear cells + delicate vessels + renal-vein invasion.
    • Wilms: tan-gray childhood renal mass + triphasic blastema/epithelium/stroma.
    • BPH: transition-zone nodules + two-layered glands + basal cells preserved.
    • Prostate carcinoma: peripheral small glands + basal-cell loss + perineural invasion + osteoblastic metastases.
    • Seminoma: homogeneous fleshy mass + clear cells + lymphocytic septa; pure seminoma does not raise AFP.
    • Yolk-sac tumor: Schiller-Duval bodies + AFP.
    • Choriocarcinoma: cytotrophoblast + syncytiotrophoblast + hemorrhage + hCG.
    • Endometriosis: chocolate cyst + endometrial glands/stroma ± hemosiderin.
    • Cervical HSIL: p16 block positivity supports lesion, but stromal invasion establishes carcinoma.
    • Leiomyoma: firm gray-white whorled mass + bland smooth muscle.
    • Leiomyosarcoma: atypia + mitoses + tumor-cell necrosis; leiomyoma is not its usual precursor.
    • Ovarian serous tumors: psammoma bodies do not prove malignancy; destructive stromal invasion does.
    • Tumor-marker rule: PSA, AFP, hCG and CA-125 support diagnosis or monitoring in the appropriate context but do not replace morphology; IHC establishes lineage, while invasion/metastasis establishes malignant behavior.
    ⚖️ Endocrine Disorders
    • Pituitary adenoma: monomorphic cells + absent/disrupted reticulin; functioning tumor = hormone excess, macroadenoma = mass effect.
    • Sheehan: postpartum hemorrhage → anterior-pituitary infarction → failure to lactate/panhypopituitarism.
    • Central DI: dilute polyuria + serum hyperosmolality; central disease responds to desmopressin.
    • Graves: diffuse meaty goiter + scalloped colloid + papillary infoldings without fibrovascular cores → T4/T3↑, TSH↓.
    • Hashimoto: Hürthle cells + lymphoid follicles/germinal centers → TSH↑, anti-TPO+.
    • Papillary thyroid CA: Orphan Annie nuclei + grooves + pseudoinclusions ± psammoma bodies → lymphatic spread.
    • Follicular thyroid CA: capsular/vascular invasion = carcinoma; FNA cannot make this distinction.
    • Medullary thyroid CA: calcitonin + Congo-red-positive amyloid + salt-and-pepper neuroendocrine cells.
    • Anaplastic thyroid CA: elderly patient + rapidly enlarging invasive neck mass + bizarre spindle/giant cells.
    • Parathyroid adenoma: single tan-red gland + chief cells + compressed normal rim → PTH↑, Ca↑, phosphate↓.
    • Parathyroid hyperplasia: multiple glands enlarged.
    • Parathyroid carcinoma: invasion/metastasis—not endocrine atypia—proves malignancy.
    • Cushing: ACTH excess → bilateral adrenal hyperplasia; exogenous steroids → bilateral cortical atrophy; Crooke change signals glucocorticoid excess.
    • Conn: small yellow cortical adenoma + aldosterone↑/renin↓ ± hypokalemic alkalosis.
    • Addison: small shrunken autoimmune adrenals + cortical destruction + preserved medulla → cortisol↓/ACTH↑ + hyperpigmentation.
    • Pheochromocytoma: zellballen + chromogranin/synaptophysin + S-100 sustentacular cells → metanephrines↑; pleomorphism does not prove malignancy.
    • Adrenal adenoma: small homogeneous yellow; adrenal carcinoma: large invasive variegated hemorrhagic-necrotic tumor.
    • T1DM: insulitis + β-cell loss; T2DM: islet amyloid, but amyloid is not specific.
    • PanNET: organoid nests/cords + salt-and-pepper nuclei + chromogranin/synaptophysin.
    • Insulinoma: hypoglycemia with inappropriate endogenous insulin elevation.
    • Gastrinoma: fasting gastrin↑ + multiple/refractory peptic ulcers + diarrhea.
    • Final malignancy rule: IHC establishes endocrine lineage; invasion/metastasis or disease-specific invasive criteria establish malignant behavior. Hormone excess establishes functionality—not malignancy.
    🧠 Nervous System Disorders
    • Cerebral infarction: red neurons → neutrophils → macrophages → gliosis → cystic cavity; brain infarction undergoes liquefactive necrosis.
    • Lacunar infarct: <15-mm deep cavitary infarct from hypertensive penetrating-vessel disease.
    • Hypertension: deep putaminal/thalamic/pontine hemorrhage + hyaline arteriolosclerosis.
    • CAA: lobar hemorrhage + Aβ-containing cortical/leptomeningeal vessels.
    • Saccular aneurysm: thin-walled circle-of-Willis branch-point aneurysm → spontaneous SAH.
    • Edema: flattened gyri + narrowed sulci; cytotoxic = cellular, vasogenic = BBB leakage, interstitial = hydrocephalic CSF flow.
    • Hydrocephalus: ventricles dilate; edema usually compresses them.
    • Herniation: subfalcine → ACA; uncal → CN III/PCA/brainstem; tonsillar → medulla.
    • Pyogenic meningitis: purulent leptomeninges + subarachnoid neutrophils + CSF glucose↓.
    • Brain abscess: central liquefactive necrosis → vascular granulation tissue → collagenous capsule → outer gliosis.
    • HSV: hemorrhagic temporal-lobe necrosis + lymphocytic cuffs + microglial nodules ± Cowdry A inclusions.
    • TB meningitis: basal granulomatous meningitis + low-glucose lymphocytic CSF.
    • MS: active macrophage-rich demyelinating plaque + Luxol-fast-blue myelin loss + relative axonal preservation.
    • Alzheimer: cortical/hippocampal atrophy + neuritic Aβ plaques + tau tangles + hydrocephalus ex vacuo.
    • Parkinson: pale substantia nigra + α-synuclein Lewy bodies.
    • CJD: spongiform vacuolation + rapidly progressive dementia.
    • Glioblastoma: hemorrhagic-necrotic infiltrative mass + pseudopalisading necrosis + microvascular proliferation.
    • Oligodendroglioma: fried-egg cells + chicken-wire vessels + calcification, integrated with IDH/1p19q status.
    • Ependymoma: perivascular pseudorosettes + dot/ring EMA, often fourth ventricular in children.
    • Medulloblastoma: cerebellar small-blue-cell tumor + Homer-Wright rosettes + CSF drop metastases.
    • Meningioma: dural-based compressive mass + whorls + psammoma bodies + EMA.
    • Arterial occlusion → ATP depletion/excitotoxic injury → red neurons/liquefactive infarction → abrupt focal deficit → MRI vascular-territory lesion.
    • Chronic hypertension → penetrating-vessel hyalinosis → deep lacune → pure motor/sensory syndrome → small deep infarct on imaging.
    • Hypertensive vessel rupture → deep hematoma → internal-capsule/basal-ganglia destruction → sudden hemiparesis ± ICP elevation.
    • CAA → amyloid-weakened cortical vessels → lobar hemorrhage → recurrent focal neurologic deficits → Aβ-positive vessels on tissue.
    • Saccular aneurysm rupture → arterial blood in subarachnoid space → meningeal irritation/ICP surge → thunderclap headache → CT/CSF evidence of SAH.
    • BBB disruption → vasogenic edema → increased brain volume → headache/papilledema → imaging shows perilesional edema.
    • CSF outflow/resorption failure → ventricular dilation → ICP ↑ → headache/vomiting → imaging localizes obstructive versus communicating hydrocephalus.
    • Expanding mass/edema → pressure gradient → uncal herniation → CN III compression → ipsilateral fixed dilated pupil.
    • Pyogenic infection → neutrophilic leptomeningitis → neck stiffness/altered consciousness → CSF neutrophils↑, protein↑, glucose↓.
    • Parenchymal suppuration → liquefactive abscess + vasogenic edema → headache/focal deficit → ring lesion with infectious morphology/culture.
    • HSV replication → hemorrhagic temporal necrosis → behavioral change/seizures → CSF HSV PCR positivity.
    • TB granulomatous basilar meningitis → arterial/CSF-pathway compromise → cranial neuropathy/infarct/hydrocephalus → lymphocytic low-glucose CSF + microbiologic confirmation.
    • Immune attack on myelin → plaques with early axonal preservation → relapsing multifocal deficits → MRI dissemination + CSF oligoclonal bands.
    • Aβ/tau accumulation → hippocampal/cortical neuronal loss → progressive memory/cognitive decline → CSF Aβ↓/phospho-tau↑ with characteristic neuropathology.
    • Nigral neuron loss → dopamine depletion → bradykinesia/rigidity/tremor → pale substantia nigra + α-synuclein Lewy bodies.
    • Prion propagation → spongiform neuronal injury → rapidly progressive dementia/myoclonus → RT-QuIC SUPPLEMENTARY + spongiform pathology.
    • Glioblastoma angiogenesis/necrosis → infiltrative hemorrhagic tumor + edema → seizures/focal deficits/ICP → tissue shows pseudopalisading necrosis and microvascular proliferation.
    • Oligodendroglioma cortical infiltration → slow-growing calcified hemispheric tumor → seizures → fried-egg/chicken-wire morphology + diagnostic molecular profile.
    • Fourth-ventricular ependymoma → CSF obstruction → hydrocephalus → vomiting/headache → perivascular pseudorosettes with GFAP/EMA.
    • Cerebellar medulloblastoma → rapid embryonal growth → fourth-ventricle obstruction/ataxia → small-blue-cell/Homer-Wright morphology + possible CSF drop metastases.
    • Dural meningothelial growth → extra-axial compression → seizures/focal dysfunction → whorls/psammoma bodies with EMA-positive tumor.
    • Final PLE synthesis: with 4 Applying + 1 Analyzing + 1 Evaluating item, prioritize anatomic location → dominant tissue reaction → time course → stain/CSF/imaging confirmation, then decide whether the finding is diagnostic, merely characteristic, or a common distractor.

    📋One-Look Morphology Grids — All Systems

    🔬 Compressed Rapid-Review
    Disease/processGross → MicroConfirm / labMimic killer
    Reversible injurySwollen/pale → hydropic swellingH&E; no specific labIntact nucleus/membrane
    Coagulative necrosisPale/firm → ghost architectureH&E; organ marker ↑Architecture preserved
    Liquefactive necrosisSoft/liquid → architecture destroyedH&EComplete digestion
